<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>A</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> be a symmetric $`n\times n`$ matrix whose entries on and above the diagonal are independent uniform signs. We prove
> 
> $`\displaystyle \Pr(\det A_n=0)=\left(\frac12+o(1)\right)^n.`$

- **claim-sharp-symmetric-singularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For the symmetric Bernoulli matrix $A_n$, $$\Pr(\det A_n=0)=\left(\frac12+o(1)\right)^n =2^{-n+o(n)} \qquad\text{as }n\to\infty.$$
  - Explanation: The symmetric uniform-sign ensemble has the same leading exponential singularity scale as the event that a specified pair of rows agrees.
- **claim-sign-to-bit-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sign-bit)): For $n\ge2$, $$\Pr(\det A_n=0)=\Pr(\det\mathbf B_{n-1}=0).$$
  - Explanation: Diagonal congruence and a Schur complement transfer singularity exactly from the sign ensemble to the binary ensemble in the smaller dimension.
- **claim-full-rank-principal-minor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-principal-rank)): Let $M$ be a symmetric matrix over a field, of rank $r$. Then $M$ has a nonsingular principal submatrix of size $r$.
  - Explanation: Symmetry guarantees a nonsingular principal submatrix whose size equals the matrix rank, over any field.
- **claim-field-uniform-corank-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-corank-tail)): Let $\mathbb K$ be any field, and regard the bits of $\mathbf B_m$ as elements of $\mathbb K$. For $1\le k\le m$, $$\Pr\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb K}\mathbf B_m\ge k\bigr) \le \sum_{j=k}^{m}\binom mj\,2^{-j(j+1)/2}.$$ Consequently, uniformly for $m\in\mathcal I_N$, $$\Pr\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb K}\mathbf B_m>N^{3/5}\bigr) \le 2^{-\Omega(N^{6/5})}.$$ The implicit constant in the last bound is independent of the field.
  - Explanation: A large rank deficiency forces many independent entries of a complementary symmetric block. Counting the possible principal blocks gives a field-independent tail bound.
- **claim-reduction-to-corank-one** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-corank-reduction)): For the binary model in dimension $N$, $$\Pr(\det\mathbf B_N=0) \le 2^{o(N)}\Pr(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}\mathbf B_N=1) +2^{-\Omega(N^{6/5})}.$$
  - Explanation: Flipping selected diagonal bits reduces a singular matrix to corank one with subexponential multiplicity; the larger-corank tail supplies the remainder.
- **claim-admissible-column-reduction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-column-reduction)): One has $$\Pr(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}\mathbf B_N=1) \le N\,2^{-N}\,\mathbb E\|\mathcal S(\mathbf B_{N-1})\|.$$
  - Explanation: Deleting a largest kernel coordinate leaves a nonsingular principal minor and an admissible column. A union over the deleted coordinate yields the expectation bound.
- **claim-static-admissible-potential** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-admissible-potential)): For every fixed $A>0$, typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, $$\|\mathcal S(B)\|\le 2^{N(\Psi(B)+\ell^{-A})}.$$
  - Explanation: On a typical nonsingular matrix, the discriminant-group potential controls the logarithmic size of the admissible-column set.
- **claim-potential-exponential-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-path-potential)): For a binary matrix of size $N-1$, $$\mathbb E\!\left[\mathbf 1_{\{\det\mathbf B_{N-1}\ne0\}}\, 2^{N\Psi(\mathbf B_{N-1})}\right]\le 2^{o(N)}.$$
  - Explanation: The exponential potential weight has subexponential expectation when restricted to nonsingular binary matrices.
- **claim-orthogonal-lattice-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice-quotient)): Let $L$ have rank $r$ and span $E$, and let $K\subset L$ be a primitive sublattice of rank $k$, with $F=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}K$. Write $\pi_{F^\perp}$ for orthogonal projection from $E$ onto $E\cap F^\perp$. The Euclidean realization $$L/K:=\pi_{F^\perp}(L)$$ is a lattice of rank $r-k$, canonically isomorphic to the abstract quotient, and $$\det L=\det K\,\det(L/K),\qquad \det L^*=(\det L)^{-1}.$$ The dual identities are $$(L/K)^*=L^*\cap F^\perp,\qquad K^*=\pi_F(L^*),$$ where $\pi_F$ is orthogonal projection onto $F$. For any sublattice $M\subset L/K$, its full preimage $\widetilde M=\{x\in L:\pi_{F^\perp}x\in M\}$ has rank $k+\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M$ and satisfies $$\det\widetilde M=\det K\,\det M.$$ These assertions include $k=0$, $k=r$, and $r=0$, with all rank-zero determinants equal to one.
  - Explanation: A primitive sublattice admits an orthogonal quotient with determinant factorization, compatible dual identities, and a determinant formula for lifted sublattices. The conventions include all rank-zero cases.
- **claim-rational-height-submodularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-height-submodularity)): For rational subspaces $U,V\subset\mathbb R^m$, $$H(U+V)H(U\cap V)\le H(U)H(V).$$ Consequently $h(U+V)+h(U\cap V)\le h(U)+h(V)$. Every rational subspace has height at least one, and, for fixed $m$ and $C<\infty$, there are only finitely many rational subspaces $W\subset\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\le C$. These statements include rank-zero subspaces.
  - Explanation: Rational height is multiplicatively submodular and its normalized logarithm is additively submodular. Bounded height permits only finitely many rational subspaces in a fixed ambient dimension.
- **claim-normal-lattice-height-ratio** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normal-height)): Let $V\subset U\subset\mathbb R^m$ be rational subspaces, and put $\Lambda=\Lambda_U^*$. The primitive normal lattice $$\mathcal N_U(V)=\Lambda\cap V^\perp$$ has rank $\dim U-\dim V$, and $$\pi_V(\Lambda)=\Lambda_V^*,\qquad \det\mathcal N_U(V)=\frac{H(V)}{H(U)}.$$ More generally, for any sublattice $M\subset\Lambda$, set $V_M=U\cap(\mathop{\mathrm{span}}M)^\perp$. Then $V_M$ is rational and $$\det M=[\mathcal N_U(V_M):M]\frac{H(V_M)}{H(U)} \ge \frac1{H(U)}.$$ For $V=U$ the normal lattice has rank zero and determinant one; for $V=\{0\}$ it is $\Lambda_U^*$. If $M=\{0\}$, the displayed index is one and the determinant formula gives one.
  - Explanation: The determinant of a primitive normal lattice is the height ratio of nested rational spaces. Saturation extends this identity to arbitrary sublattices of the dual.
- **claim-reverse-minkowski-gaussian-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski)): Let $L$ be a lattice of rank $j\ge1$, and let $T>0$. Suppose that $$\det M\ge T^{\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M}\qquad\text{for every sublattice }M\subset L.$$ With $\tau_j=10(\log j+2)$, one has $$\rho_{T/\tau_j}(L)\le\frac32.$$ For a rank-zero lattice the Gaussian mass is one at every scale, so the corresponding assertion is immediate.
  - Explanation: A determinant lower bound for every sublattice controls Gaussian mass at the prescribed logarithmically reduced scale. The rank-zero case follows separately from the conventions.
- **claim-gaussian-lattice-point-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-count)): Let $L$ be a rank-$j$ lattice. For $R\ge0$ and $s>0$, $$\#\{x\in L:\\|x\\|\le R\} \le \exp(\pi R^2/s^2)\rho_s(L).$$ For $s'\ge s>0$, $$\rho_{s'}(L)\le (s'/s)^j\rho_s(L).$$ In particular, under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:reverse-minkowski" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:reverse-minkowski">12</a>, if $s_0=T/\tau_j$, then for every $u\ge s_0$, $$\#\{x\in L:\\|x\\|\le R\} \le\frac32\left(\frac{u}{s_0}\right)^j \exp(\pi R^2/u^2).$$ When $j=0$, the point count and every Gaussian mass are one; the first inequality is immediate and the second is equality.
  - Explanation: Gaussian mass bounds the number of lattice points in a Euclidean ball. Poisson summation controls changes of scale and transfers the Reverse Minkowski estimate to larger scales.
- **claim-normalized-determinant-minimizer** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-det-minimizer)): Let $L$ be a lattice and let $T>0$. The quantity $$\frac{\det M}{T^{\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M}},\qquad M\subset L\text{ a sublattice},$$ has a minimum, attained by a primitive sublattice $L_0$. It satisfies $$\det L_0\le T^{\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}L_0},\qquad \det M\ge T^{\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M}\quad\text{for every sublattice }M\subset L/L_0.$$ If in addition $T_0>T$, $\Delta\ge0$, and $$\det M\ge e^{-\Delta}T_0^{\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M} \quad\text{for every sublattice }M\subset L,$$ then $$\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}L_0\le\frac{\Delta}{\log(T_0/T)}.$$ For $L=\{0\}$ the unique choice is $L_0=\{0\}$, and every assertion holds with rank zero and determinant one.
  - Explanation: A primitive minimizer of normalized determinant leaves an orthogonal quotient satisfying the stronger sublattice determinant condition. An additional lower bound limits the rank removed.
- **claim-robust-affine-cube-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-robust-extraction)): Let $\varnothing\ne\mathcal A\subset\{0,1\}^m$, let $\rho>0$, and put $d=\dim\mathop{\mathrm{aff}}\mathcal A$. There is an affine restriction $\mathcal A_0=\mathcal A\cap F$, with $F=\mathop{\mathrm{aff}}\mathcal A_0$ of dimension $r$, such that $\mathcal A_0$ is $\rho$-robust and $$\log_2\|\mathcal A_0\| \ge \log_2\|\mathcal A\|-\rho(d-r) \ge \log_2\|\mathcal A\|-\rho m, \qquad \log_2\|\mathcal A_0\|\le r.$$ For any fixed $a_0\in\mathcal A_0$, the number of ordered $r$-tuples $(a_1,\ldots,a_r)\in\mathcal A_0^r$ for which $a_1-a_0,\ldots,a_r-a_0$ are independent is at least $$\|\mathcal A_0\|^r\prod_{t=1}^r(1-2^{-\rho t}) \ge \bigl((1-2^{-\rho})\|\mathcal A_0\|\bigr)^r.$$ When $r=0$, $\mathcal A_0$ is a singleton and the number of empty ordered tuples is one.
  - Explanation: Maximizing size penalized by affine dimension extracts a robust affine restriction while controlling size loss. Robustness also gives many ordered independent difference tuples.
- **claim-robust-image-lattice-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-robust-height)): Fix $D>0$ and $C_0>0$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $\rho=\ell^{-D}$. Let $m\le N$, let $\varnothing\ne\mathcal A\subset\{0,1\}^m$ be $\rho$-robust on its affine span $F$ of dimension $r$, and let $\Phi:F\longrightarrow\mathbb R^d$ be an injective rational affine map, meaning that it maps rational points of $F$ to rational points of $\mathbb R^d$. Suppose that $$\\|\Phi(a)-\Phi(b)\\|\le C_0\sqrt N \qquad(a,b\in\mathcal A).$$ For any $a_0\in\mathcal A$, the group $$L_\Phi=\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}\mathbb Z\bigl(\Phi(a)-\Phi(a_0)\bigr)$$ is a rank-$r$ lattice, independent of the choice of $a_0$, and $$\det L_\Phi\le \exp\bigl(O_{D,C_0}(N\ell)\bigr).$$ In particular, with $W=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(\mathcal A-\mathcal A)$, $$H(W)\le \exp\bigl(O_D(N\ell)\bigr).$$ If $r=0$, both lattices in question have rank zero and determinant one.
  - Explanation: Short differences of a robust cube set under a rational affine injection generate a lattice of controlled determinant. The identity map yields a height bound for the difference span.
- **claim-low-height-rank-distance-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-low-height-cover)): Fix $D,A>0$. For all sufficiently large $N$, and every integer $0\le m\le N$, there is a list $\mathcal C_{m,N}$ of rational subspaces of $\mathbb R^m$, depending also on $D,A$, such that $$\|\mathcal C_{m,N}\|\le 2^{N^2\ell^{-A}}.$$ Every rational subspace $W\subseteq\mathbb R^m$ satisfying $H(W)\le\exp(N\ell^D)$ has $$\min_{U\in\mathcal C_{m,N}}d(U,W)\le N\ell^{-A}.$$
  - Explanation: A fixed finite list approximates every rational subspace in the stated height range by rank distance. The list is chosen independently of the subspace being approximated.
- **claim-simultaneous-low-height-ranks** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-typical-ranks)): For every pair of fixed constants $D,A>0$, there is a function $f_{D,A}(N)\to\infty$ such that the following holds for every $m\in\mathcal I_N$ and all sufficiently large $N$, with probability at least $1-2^{-Nf_{D,A}(N)}$ for $B=\mathbf B_m$. Simultaneously for every rational subspace $W\subseteq\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\le\exp(N\ell^D)$, one has* 1. *$\dim W^\perp-\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(B\|_{W^\perp})\le N\ell^{-A}$;* 2. *for every coordinate set $X\subseteq[m]$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\bigl[\pi_{W^\perp}Be_i\bigr]_{i\in X} \ge \min\{\|X\|,m-\dim W\}-N\ell^{-A}.$$* *The ranks and the orthogonal projections in these conclusions are over $\mathbb R$; no nonsingularity assumption on $B$ is needed.
  - Explanation: A finite cover and finite-field rank tests give simultaneous real rank bounds for all low-height rational spaces and all coordinate sets. Nonsingularity is unnecessary.
- **claim-low-height-inverse-form-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-inverse-rank)): For every pair of fixed constants $D,A>0$, typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, simultaneously for every rational $W\subseteq\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\le\exp(N\ell^D)$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(B^{-1}\|_W)\ge\dim W-N\ell^{-A}.$$ Here $B^{-1}\|_W$ is the restriction of the bilinear form with matrix $B^{-1}$, rather than the restriction of its associated linear map. The exceptional probability is uniform in $m$, as in Proposition <a href="#prop:typical-ranks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:typical-ranks">18</a>.
  - Explanation: For a nonsingular matrix, the radical of its inverse form on a subspace is isomorphic to the radical of the original form on the orthogonal complement. The simultaneous rank bound therefore transfers to the inverse bilinear form.
- **claim-simultaneous-prime-corank** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-prime-corank)): Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, one has, simultaneously for every prime $p$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb F_p}(B\bmod p)\le N^{3/5}.$$ More precisely, uniformly for $m\in\mathcal I_N$, the probability of nonsingularity together with failure of this assertion is at most $2^{-\Omega(N^{6/5})}$.
  - Explanation: Only primes dividing the nonzero determinant can have positive corank. Combining the determinant bound with the field-uniform corank tail controls every prime simultaneously.
- **claim-typical-small-spectrum** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-typical-spectrum)): For every fixed $A>0$, there is a fixed $C_A>0$ and a function $f_A(N)\to\infty$ such that, for every $m\in\mathcal I_N$ and all sufficiently large $N$, with probability at least $1-2^{-Nf_A(N)}$, the number of eigenvalues of $B=\mathbf B_m$ in $$[-\sqrt N\,\ell^{-C_A},\ \sqrt N\,\ell^{-C_A}]$$ is at most $N\ell^{-A}$. Eigenvalues are counted with multiplicity. One may take $C_A=A+6$. This assertion does not require $B$ to be nonsingular.
  - Explanation: Closed-walk moments and convex trace concentration limit the number of eigenvalues near zero. A rank-one perturbation transfers the estimate from centered signs to binary matrices; eigenvalues are counted with multiplicity.
- **claim-perfect-primary-pairing-blocks** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-discriminant-blocks)): Let $G$ be a finite abelian $p$-group with a perfect symmetric pairing $\lambda:G\times G\to\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$. It is an orthogonal direct sum of blocks of the following forms:* 1. *a cyclic group of order $p^j$ whose generator has self-pairing $a/p^j\bmod\mathbb Z$, with $p\nmid a$;* 2. *when $p=2$, a group $(\mathbb Z/2^j\mathbb Z)^2$ with pairing matrix $$2^{-j}\begin{pmatrix}2a&c\\c&2d\end{pmatrix}\bmod\mathbb Z, \qquad c\ \text{odd}.$$
  - Explanation: A perfect symmetric pairing on a finite primary group splits orthogonally into cyclic blocks and, at the even prime, the stated blocks with even diagonal numerators and odd off-diagonal numerator.
- **claim-isotropic-primary-coloring** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-primary-coloring)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#lem:discriminant-blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:discriminant-blocks">22</a>, put $h=b_3$, with zero exponents appended as necessary. In an orthogonal block decomposition, let $U$ be the sum of the blocks of exponent strictly greater than $h$, and let $T$ be the remaining sum. There is a coloring of the isotropic elements of $G$ with $C_p$ colors such that elements $x,y$ of one color satisfy $$\label{eq:primary-color-pairing} p^h\lambda_U(x_U,y_U)=0\quad\text{in }\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z.$$ Here $x_U,y_U$ denote the projections to $U$. The color counts may be chosen to satisfy $$C_p\le 1+8b_1,\qquad C_p\le 2^{b_2-b_3}\quad\text{if \(p\) is odd}.$$
  - Explanation: The cutoff at the third invariant exponent leaves at most two cyclic factors above it. Explicit colors control mutual pairing denominators on that part, with distinct bounds for odd and even primes.
- **claim-monochromatic-pairing-image-order** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-colored-image-order)): Let $X$ be a set of isotropic elements of $G_B$ in a single overall color, and let $$H=\langle x-y:x,y\in X\rangle\subset G_B.$$ For the restricted pairing map $$\theta_H:H\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(H,\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z),\qquad \theta_H(u)=\lambda_B(u,\cdot)\|_H,$$ one has $$\|\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\theta_H\|\le N^{NJ_B}.$$
  - Explanation: Differences of isotropic elements in one overall color generate a subgroup whose restricted pairing image is controlled by the layers of height at least three.
- **claim-pairing-index-determinant-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pairing-determinant)): Let $L$ be a lattice, let $V=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}L$, and let $q$ be a symmetric bilinear form on $V$ such that $q(L,L)\subset\mathbb Q$. Write $$K=L\cap\operatorname{rad}q,\qquad S=V\cap(\operatorname{rad}q)^\perp,\qquad \bar L=\operatorname{proj}_S L.$$ Then $K$ spans the radical and is primitive in $L$, and $\bar L$ is a lattice in $S$. If $t$ is the order of the image of $$L\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(L,\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z),\qquad x\longmapsto q(x,\cdot)\bmod\mathbb Z,$$ then $$\label{eq:pairing-determinant} \left\|\det_{\mathrm{orth}}(q\|_S)\right\| \ge \frac{1}{t(\det\bar L)^2} =\frac{(\det K)^2}{t(\det L)^2}.$$ Here the determinant on the left is taken in an orthonormal basis of $S$, and all rank-zero determinants are one.
  - Explanation: After removing the rational radical, integrality on the kernel of the pairing modulo integers gives a determinant lower bound involving the pairing image order and Euclidean lattice covolume.
- **claim-gated-principal-deletion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-gated-deletion)): Let $B$ be a nonsingular symmetric $r\times r$ matrix with entries in $\{0,1\}$, where $r\ge1$. Either a principal submatrix obtained by deleting one coordinate is nonsingular, or there are distinct coordinates $i,j$ such that, with $I=[r]\setminus\{i,j\}$, $$C=B_{I,I}\quad\hbox{is nonsingular},\qquad v_1=B_{I,i}\in\mathcal S(C),\quad v_2=B_{I,j}\in\mathcal S(C).$$ For the empty minor we use determinant one, and $\mathcal S$ contains its unique empty column.
  - Explanation: A nonzero inverse diagonal entry permits deletion of a coordinate. Otherwise a largest inverse off-diagonal entry permits deletion of a pair, and both removed cross columns satisfy admissibility for the remaining minor.
- **claim-conditional-potential-step** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-potential-step)): For every fixed $A>0$, the following holds typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$. For each $h\in\{1,2\}$ with $m+h\in\mathcal I_N$, the random extension in Equation <a href="#eq:random-extension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:random-extension">[eq:random-extension]</a> satisfies $$\label{eq:potential-step} \mathbb E\!\left[ \mathbf 1_{\{\det B\ne0\}}\mathbf 1_{\mathcal G_h}\,2^{N\Psi(B)} \,\middle\|\, C\right] \le 2^{N(\Psi(C)+\ell^{-A})}.$$ The typical condition is imposed only on the starting matrix $C$.
  - Explanation: A local conditional exponential-moment estimate pays for potential growth from a typical starting matrix. Width one uses certificates and scalar budgets; width two additionally imposes the admissible-column gate.
- **claim-one-element-layer-interlacing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layer-quotient)): Let $G$ be a finite abelian group, let $v\in G$, and put $H=G/\langle v\rangle$. Fix a prime $p$, and define $u_t$ by $$p^{u_t}=\|\langle v+p^tG\rangle\|\quad\hbox{in }G/p^tG \qquad(t\ge0).$$ Then $u_0=0$, $u_t-u_{t-1}\in\{0,1\}$ for $t\ge1$, and $$\begin{aligned} F_H(p,t)&=F_G(p,t)-u_t,\label{eq:quotient-prefix}\\ k_H(p,e)&=k_G(p,e)-(u_e-u_{e-1})\in \{k_G(p,e),k_G(p,e)-1\}.\label{eq:layer-interlacing} \end{aligned}$$ Call a positive layer $(p,e)$ of $G$ *retained* if $k_H(p,e)=k_G(p,e)$. On every maximal interval of consecutive layers where $k_G(p,e)$ has a fixed positive value, the retained layers form an initial interval, possibly empty. We call such an interval of constant height a *plateau*.
  - Explanation: Quotienting a finite abelian group by an element decreases each primary layer height by at most one. Retention on each constant-height plateau is an initial interval.
- **claim-retention-forced-integer-lattice** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-retention-lattice)): Let $C$ be a nonsingular binary matrix of size $m$, and let $\pi:\mathbb Z^m\twoheadrightarrow H$ be a quotient map to a finite abelian group such that $C\mathbb Z^m\subseteq\ker\pi$. Let $E$ be any set of positive layers of $H$. There is a lattice $K_E$, depending only on $\pi,H,E$, with $$\label{eq:retention-lattice} C\mathbb Z^m\subseteq K_E\subseteq\mathbb Z^m,\qquad [\mathbb Z^m:K_E]=N^{Nq(E)},\qquad q(E)=\sum_{(p,e)\in E} k_H(p,e)\nu_p,$$ such that the following implication holds for every $v\in\mathbb Z^m$: if every layer in $E$ is retained in $H\longrightarrow H/\langle\pi(v)\rangle$, then $v\in K_E$.
  - Explanation: Selected retained layers impose coordinate divisibility in the finite quotient. Their inverse image is an intermediate integer lattice with the stated exact index; membership is a necessary condition for retention.
- **claim-symmetric-prime-power-kernel-inequality** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-layer-inequality)): For a nonsingular extension $B$ of a nonsingular $C$ of width $h\in\{1,2\}$, at every prime $p$ and every integer $t\ge0$, $$\label{eq:layer-inequality} F_B(p,t)\le F_C(p,t)+ht-2U_t.$$
  - Explanation: Symmetry identifies the annihilator of the old kernel with the old image. Counting both block equations then bounds the destination layer prefix using the total quotient loss.
- **claim-simultaneous-cube-coset-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cube-concentration)): For every fixed $A>0$, typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, one has simultaneously for every lattice $B\mathbb Z^m\subseteq K\subseteq\mathbb Z^m$ $$\label{eq:cube-concentration} M_K\le 2^{N(1-c(q_K)+\ell^{-A})}.$$ The typical event may depend on $A$, but it does not depend on $K$. Thus the choice of $K$ may depend arbitrarily on $B$ and on any other data.
  - Explanation: One typical event bounds the largest cube intersection with every coset of every intermediate lattice. The lattice may be selected after the matrix and other data are known.
- **claim-low-height-exact-basis-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-basis-count)): Fix $C,C'>0$. Uniformly for $m\in\mathcal I_N$ and $0\le j\le m$, the number of ordered tuples $$(y,y_1,\ldots,y_j)\in(\{0,1\}^m)^{j+1}$$ for which $y_1-y,\ldots,y_j-y$ are independent and their span $W$ satisfies $H(W)\le\exp(N\ell^C)$ is at most $$2^{j^2+o(N^2\ell^{-C'})}.$$
  - Explanation: Encoding pivot entries, exceptional coordinates, and normal-lattice quotient images bounds the number of ordered cube difference bases with controlled rational height.
- **claim-robust-witness-pricing** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-witness-pricing)): Fix $D_0>0$, and put $\eta=\ell^{-D_0}$ and $\rho=\eta^3$. Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, the following assertion holds simultaneously for every $B\mathbb Z^m\subseteq K\subseteq\mathbb Z^m$.* *Let $S_1,\ldots,S_h\subset\{0,1\}^m$ be nonempty subsets, each $\rho$-robust on its affine span, where $h\le3/\eta$. Suppose $S_i-S_i\subset K$, and put $W_i=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(S_i-S_i)$. Suppose also that $W=\sum_i W_i$ is a direct sum. Write $$\dim W_i=t_iN,\qquad t=\sum_i t_i,\qquad \|S_i\|=2^{\alpha_iN}.$$ If $D\subset[m]$ has $\|D\|=xN$, $x\le t$, and $M_K(D)\le2^{\eta N}$, then $$\label{eq:witness-pricing} xt-\frac{x^2}{2} \le \sum_i t_i(t_i-\alpha_i)+\eta t+\eta^3.$$
  - Explanation: For robust cube sets with independent difference spans, the savings forced on matrix rows cannot exceed the cost of specifying their bases, after accounting for basis multiplicity and the stated errors.
- **claim-retained-layer-certificate-pricing** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-certificate-pricing)): Fix $A>0$ and at most two positive constants $w_i$. Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, the following assertion holds. The typical set is obtained by imposing Proposition <a href="#prop:cube-concentration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:cube-concentration">31</a> at a sufficiently large fixed precision depending on $A$ and these constants.* *Let $\mathcal F$ denote exposed information which fixes $C$, a quotient map $G_C\twoheadrightarrow G$, and the data described next. Suppose that, conditionally on $\mathcal F$, a tested column $v$ is uniform on $\{0,1\}^m$. Partition the layers of $G$ into at most two categories, each a union of whole plateaus. In category $i$, assume that every height is at least $w_i$; only $w_i=1,2$ will be needed. The partition is part of the exposed data. In the quotient of $G$ by the image of $v$, an outcome may mark some of the retained layers. Require that the marked layers on each plateau form a prefix. The marks may also depend on other random bits in the experiment.* *Let $\mathcal A$ be any subevent of this experiment. There is a partition of $\mathcal A$ into at most $2^{N\ell^{-A}}$ classes, constructed before choosing a representative or any target weights, with the following property. Take any nonempty class $\mathcal A_\alpha$, any representative outcome in it, and write $s_i$ and $u_i$ for the representative’s sums of the weights $\nu_p$ of the marked and unmarked layers in category $i$. For any choices $0\le x_i\le s_i$, $$\label{eq:certificate-pricing} \Pr(\mathcal A_\alpha\mid\mathcal F) \le 2^{-N\left( c\left(\sum_i w_i x_i\right) -\sum_i\mathcal E(s_i,u_i,x_i)-\ell^{-A}\right)}.$$ Here $\mathcal A_\alpha$ already includes the restriction to $\mathcal A$; the probability is conditioned only on the exposed information. The class count and the estimate are uniform in that information, the quotient, the subevent, and the representative. In particular, the exposed information may include a preceding column that fixes $G$, provided the tested column remains conditionally uniform.
  - Explanation: A partition fixed before representative and target selection prices outcome-dependent prefix marks by fixed retention certificates. Conditional uniformity of the tested column suffices even when marks depend on other bits.
- **claim-noticeable-retention-probability-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-retention-cost)): Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, the following estimate holds. Let $h\in\{1,2\}$ and let $v_1,\ldots,v_h$ be independent uniform columns in $\{0,1\}^m$, conditionally on $C$. Starting with $G^{(0)}=G_C$, let $G^{(j)}$ be the quotient of $G^{(j-1)}$ by the image of $v_j$. For each $1\le j\le h$, and conditionally on any values of the preceding columns, $$\label{eq:retention-cost} \Pr\!\left( \sum_{\substack{(p,e)\text{ retained in}\\ G^{(j-1)}\twoheadrightarrow G^{(j)}}}\nu_p \ge\sigma \,\middle\|\, C,v_1,\ldots,v_{j-1}\right) \le 2^{-N\sigma/2}.$$ The typical set imposes one fixed precision on $C$, independent of the values of the preceding columns. When these columns occur in a random enlargement of $C$, no nonsingularity assumption on the destination matrix is required.
  - Explanation: Retaining a noticeable total layer weight has a uniform conditional exponential cost. The same typical starting event works after any preceding column is exposed, without requiring a nonsingular destination.
- **claim-scalar-certificate-budget-compensation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-budget-optimization)): Let $c$ and $\mathcal E$ be defined by Equations <a href="#eq:cube-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cube-profile">[eq:cube-profile]</a> and <a href="#eq:entropy-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:entropy-cost">[eq:entropy-cost]</a>, and let $\beta=7/5$. If $g,v_0,a_0,u\ge0$ satisfy $$2g+3a_0+u\le\frac12,\qquad g+v_0+2a_0+2u\le\frac12,$$ then there are $x\in[0,g]$ and $y\in[0,a_0]$ for which $$\label{eq:budget-primal} c(x+2y)-\mathcal E(g,v_0,x)-\mathcal E(a_0,u,y) \ge \beta g+(6-\beta)a_0-\beta u.$$
  - Explanation: The endpoint order budgets ensure that some admissible certificate target weights make concentration minus entropy costs cover the upper bound on potential growth.
- **claim-principal-extension-tail-recursion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tail-recursion)): Let $C\to B$ be a transition of width $h\in\{1,2\}$ between nonsingular matrices. In the $i$-th successive quotient of $G_C$, write $k_{i-1}(p,e)$ for the height before that quotient and $\mathcal R_i$ for its retained layers. For every integer $s\ge h$, $$\label{eq:path-tail-recursion} W_s(B)\le W_{s+h}(C) +2\sum_{i=1}^{h} \sum_{\substack{(p,e)\in\mathcal R_i\\ k_{i-1}(p,e)\ge s+1-h}}\nu_p.$$
  - Explanation: A destination high-layer tail is bounded by a shifted starting tail plus retained-layer charges from the successive quotients.
- **claim-heavy-retained-layer-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-heavy-retention-cost)): Let $\mathcal F$ denote exposed information which fixes a nonsingular binary matrix $C$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$ and a quotient map $\pi:\mathbb Z^m\twoheadrightarrow H$ with $C\mathbb Z^m\subseteq\ker\pi$. Suppose that, conditionally on $\mathcal F$, a tested column $v$ is uniform on $\{0,1\}^m$. If $\mathcal R$ is the set of layers retained on quotienting $H$ by $\pi(v)$, put $$Q=\sum_{\substack{(p,e)\in\mathcal R\\ k_H(p,e)\ge R-1}} k_H(p,e)\nu_p.$$ Fix $0<\eta<1$ and set $q_0=\eta/(100\log N)$. Uniformly for $q_0\le q\le1/2$, and for all sufficiently large $N$ depending on $\eta$, $$\label{eq:path-heavy-retention} \Pr\{Q\in[q,2q)\mid\mathcal F\}\le 2^{-3qN/5}.$$ The exposed information may include the preceding column of a width-two transition, provided the tested column remains conditionally uniform.
  - Explanation: A dissociated set bounds each fixed retention-mask probability; prefix structure limits the number of heavy masks. The resulting conditional bound needs no typicality hypothesis.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-corank-tail",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathbb K$ be any field, and regard the bits of $\\mathbf B_m$ as elements of $\\mathbb K$. For $1\\le k\\le m$, $$\\Pr\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{corank}}_{\\mathbb K}\\mathbf B_m\\ge k\\bigr)\n \\le \\sum_{j=k}^{m}\\binom mj\\,2^{-j(j+1)/2}.$$ Consequently, uniformly for $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, $$\\Pr\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{corank}}_{\\mathbb K}\\mathbf B_m>N^{3/5}\\bigr)\n \\le 2^{-\\Omega(N^{6/5})}.$$ The implicit constant in the last bound is independent of the field.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lattice-quotient",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ have rank $r$ and span $E$, and let $K\\subset L$ be a primitive sublattice of rank $k$, with $F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}K$. Write $\\pi_{F^\\perp}$ for orthogonal projection from $E$ onto $E\\cap F^\\perp$. The Euclidean realization $$L/K:=\\pi_{F^\\perp}(L)$$ is a lattice of rank $r-k$, canonically isomorphic to the abstract quotient, and $$\\det L=\\det K\\,\\det(L/K),\\qquad\n \\det L^*=(\\det L)^{-1}.$$ The dual identities are $$(L/K)^*=L^*\\cap F^\\perp,\\qquad\n K^*=\\pi_F(L^*),$$ where $\\pi_F$ is orthogonal projection onto $F$. For any sublattice $M\\subset L/K$, its full preimage $\\widetilde M=\\{x\\in L:\\pi_{F^\\perp}x\\in M\\}$ has rank $k+\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M$ and satisfies $$\\det\\widetilde M=\\det K\\,\\det M.$$ These assertions include $k=0$, $k=r$, and $r=0$, with all rank-zero determinants equal to one.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lattice-quotient",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ have rank $r$ and span $E$, and let $K\\subset L$ be a primitive sublattice of rank $k$, with $F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}K$. Write $\\pi_{F^\\perp}$ for orthogonal projection from $E$ onto $E\\cap F^\\perp$. The Euclidean realization $$L/K:=\\pi_{F^\\perp}(L)$$ is a lattice of rank $r-k$, canonically isomorphic to the abstract quotient, and $$\\det L=\\det K\\,\\det(L/K),\\qquad\n \\det L^*=(\\det L)^{-1}.$$ The dual identities are $$(L/K)^*=L^*\\cap F^\\perp,\\qquad\n K^*=\\pi_F(L^*),$$ where $\\pi_F$ is orthogonal projection onto $F$. For any sublattice $M\\subset L/K$, its full preimage $\\widetilde M=\\{x\\in L:\\pi_{F^\\perp}x\\in M\\}$ has rank $k+\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M$ and satisfies $$\\det\\widetilde M=\\det K\\,\\det M.$$ These assertions include $k=0$, $k=r$, and $r=0$, with all rank-zero determinants equal to one.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $L$ have rank $r$ and span $E$, and let $K\\subset L$ be a primitive sublattice of rank $k$, with $F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}K$. Write $\\pi_{F^\\perp}$ for orthogonal projection from $E$ onto $E\\cap F^\\perp$. The Euclidean realization $$L/K:=\\pi_{F^\\perp}(L)$$ is a lattice of rank $r-k$, canonically isomorphic to the abstract quotient, and $$\\det L=\\det K\\,\\det(L/K),\\qquad\n \\det L^*=(\\det L)^{-1}.$$ The dual identities are $$(L/K)^*=L^*\\cap F^\\perp,\\qquad\n K^*=\\pi_F(L^*),$$ where $\\pi_F$ is orthogonal projection onto $F$. For any sublattice $M\\subset L/K$, its full preimage $\\widetilde M=\\{x\\in L:\\pi_{F^\\perp}x\\in M\\}$ has rank $k+\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M$ and satisfies $$\\det\\widetilde M=\\det K\\,\\det M.$$ These assertions include $k=0$, $k=r$, and $r=0$, with all rank-zero determinants equal to one.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-submodularity",
        "exact_quote": "For rational subspaces $U,V\\subset\\mathbb R^m$, $$H(U+V)H(U\\cap V)\\le H(U)H(V).$$ Consequently $h(U+V)+h(U\\cap V)\\le h(U)+h(V)$. Every rational subspace has height at least one, and, for fixed $m$ and $C<\\infty$, there are only finitely many rational subspaces $W\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\\le C$. These statements include rank-zero subspaces.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-submodularity",
          "exact_quote": "For rational subspaces $U,V\\subset\\mathbb R^m$, $$H(U+V)H(U\\cap V)\\le H(U)H(V).$$ Consequently $h(U+V)+h(U\\cap V)\\le h(U)+h(V)$. Every rational subspace has height at least one, and, for fixed $m$ and $C<\\infty$, there are only finitely many rational subspaces $W\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\\le C$. These statements include rank-zero subspaces.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For rational subspaces $U,V\\subset\\mathbb R^m$, $$H(U+V)H(U\\cap V)\\le H(U)H(V).$$ Consequently $h(U+V)+h(U\\cap V)\\le h(U)+h(V)$. Every rational subspace has height at least one, and, for fixed $m$ and $C<\\infty$, there are only finitely many rational subspaces $W\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\\le C$. These statements include rank-zero subspaces.",
      "explanation": "Rational height is multiplicatively submodular and its normalized logarithm is additively submodular. Bounded height permits only finitely many rational subspaces in a fixed ambient dimension.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-height",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V\\subset U\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be rational subspaces, and put $\\Lambda=\\Lambda_U^*$. The primitive normal lattice $$\\mathcal N_U(V)=\\Lambda\\cap V^\\perp$$ has rank $\\dim U-\\dim V$, and $$\\pi_V(\\Lambda)=\\Lambda_V^*,\\qquad\n \\det\\mathcal N_U(V)=\\frac{H(V)}{H(U)}.$$ More generally, for any sublattice $M\\subset\\Lambda$, set $V_M=U\\cap(\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}M)^\\perp$. Then $V_M$ is rational and $$\\det M=[\\mathcal N_U(V_M):M]\\frac{H(V_M)}{H(U)}\n \\ge \\frac1{H(U)}.$$ For $V=U$ the normal lattice has rank zero and determinant one; for $V=\\{0\\}$ it is $\\Lambda_U^*$. If $M=\\{0\\}$, the displayed index is one and the determinant formula gives one.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-height",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V\\subset U\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be rational subspaces, and put $\\Lambda=\\Lambda_U^*$. Then $V_M$ is rational and $$\\det M=[\\mathcal N_U(V_M):M]\\frac{H(V_M)}{H(U)}\n \\ge \\frac1{H(U)}.$$ For $V=U$ the normal lattice has rank zero and determinant one; for $V=\\{0\\}$ it is $\\Lambda_U^*$. If $M=\\{0\\}$, the displayed index is one and the determinant formula gives one.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V\\subset U\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be rational subspaces, and put $\\Lambda=\\Lambda_U^*$. Then $V_M$ is rational and $$\\det M=[\\mathcal N_U(V_M):M]\\frac{H(V_M)}{H(U)}\n \\ge \\frac1{H(U)}.$$ For $V=U$ the normal lattice has rank zero and determinant one; for $V=\\{0\\}$ it is $\\Lambda_U^*$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-count",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a rank-$j$ lattice. For $R\\ge0$ and $s>0$, $$\\#\\{x\\in L:\\|x\\|\\le R\\}\n \\le \\exp(\\pi R^2/s^2)\\rho_s(L).$$ For $s'\\ge s>0$, $$\\rho_{s'}(L)\\le (s'/s)^j\\rho_s(L).$$ In particular, under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:reverse-minkowski\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:reverse-minkowski\">12</a>, if $s_0=T/\\tau_j$, then for every $u\\ge s_0$, $$\\#\\{x\\in L:\\|x\\|\\le R\\}\n \\le\\frac32\\left(\\frac{u}{s_0}\\right)^j\n       \\exp(\\pi R^2/u^2).$$ When $j=0$, the point count and every Gaussian mass are one; the first inequality is immediate and the second is equality.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-count",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a rank-$j$ lattice.           "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $L$ be a rank-$j$ lattice.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-det-minimizer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a lattice and let $T>0$. The quantity $$\\frac{\\det M}{T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}},\\qquad M\\subset L\\text{ a sublattice},$$ has a minimum, attained by a primitive sublattice $L_0$. It satisfies $$\\det L_0\\le T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}L_0},\\qquad\n \\det M\\ge T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}\\quad\\text{for every sublattice }M\\subset L/L_0.$$ If in addition $T_0>T$, $\\Delta\\ge0$, and $$\\det M\\ge e^{-\\Delta}T_0^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}\n       \\quad\\text{for every sublattice }M\\subset L,$$ then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}L_0\\le\\frac{\\Delta}{\\log(T_0/T)}.$$ For $L=\\{0\\}$ the unique choice is $L_0=\\{0\\}$, and every assertion holds with rank zero and determinant one.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-robust-extraction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\varnothing\\ne\\mathcal A\\subset\\{0,1\\}^m$, let $\\rho>0$, and put $d=\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{aff}}\\mathcal A$. There is an affine restriction $\\mathcal A_0=\\mathcal A\\cap F$, with $F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{aff}}\\mathcal A_0$ of dimension $r$, such that $\\mathcal A_0$ is $\\rho$-robust and $$\\log_2|\\mathcal A_0|\n \\ge \\log_2|\\mathcal A|-\\rho(d-r)\n \\ge \\log_2|\\mathcal A|-\\rho m,\n \\qquad\n \\log_2|\\mathcal A_0|\\le r.$$ For any fixed $a_0\\in\\mathcal A_0$, the number of ordered $r$-tuples $(a_1,\\ldots,a_r)\\in\\mathcal A_0^r$ for which $a_1-a_0,\\ldots,a_r-a_0$ are independent is at least $$|\\mathcal A_0|^r\\prod_{t=1}^r(1-2^{-\\rho t})\n \\ge \\bigl((1-2^{-\\rho})|\\mathcal A_0|\\bigr)^r.$$ When $r=0$, $\\mathcal A_0$ is a singleton and the number of empty ordered tuples is one.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-robust-extraction",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-robust-height",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $D>0$ and $C_0>0$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $\\rho=\\ell^{-D}$. Let $m\\le N$, let $\\varnothing\\ne\\mathcal A\\subset\\{0,1\\}^m$ be $\\rho$-robust on its affine span $F$ of dimension $r$, and let $\\Phi:F\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d$ be an injective rational affine map, meaning that it maps rational points of $F$ to rational points of $\\mathbb R^d$. Suppose that $$\\|\\Phi(a)-\\Phi(b)\\|\\le C_0\\sqrt N\n       \\qquad(a,b\\in\\mathcal A).$$ For any $a_0\\in\\mathcal A$, the group $$L_\\Phi=\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal A}\\mathbb Z\\bigl(\\Phi(a)-\\Phi(a_0)\\bigr)$$ is a rank-$r$ lattice, independent of the choice of $a_0$, and $$\\det L_\\Phi\\le \\exp\\bigl(O_{D,C_0}(N\\ell)\\bigr).$$ In particular, with $W=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}(\\mathcal A-\\mathcal A)$, $$H(W)\\le \\exp\\bigl(O_D(N\\ell)\\bigr).$$ If $r=0$, both lattices in question have rank zero and determinant one.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-robust-height",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $D>0$ and $C_0>0$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $\\rho=\\ell^{-D}$. Suppose that $$\\|\\Phi(a)-\\Phi(b)\\|\\le C_0\\sqrt N\n       \\qquad(a,b\\in\\mathcal A).$$ For any $a_0\\in\\mathcal A$, the group $$L_\\Phi=\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal A}\\mathbb Z\\bigl(\\Phi(a)-\\Phi(a_0)\\bigr)$$ is a rank-$r$ lattice, independent of the choice of $a_0$, and $$\\det L_\\Phi\\le \\exp\\bigl(O_{D,C_0}(N\\ell)\\bigr).$$ In particular, with $W=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}(\\mathcal A-\\mathcal A)$, $$H(W)\\le \\exp\\bigl(O_D(N\\ell)\\bigr).$$ If $r=0$, both lattices in question have rank zero and determinant one.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $D>0$ and $C_0>0$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $\\rho=\\ell^{-D}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-low-height-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $D,A>0$. For all sufficiently large $N$, and every integer $0\\le m\\le N$, there is a list $\\mathcal C_{m,N}$ of rational subspaces of $\\mathbb R^m$, depending also on $D,A$, such that $$|\\mathcal C_{m,N}|\\le 2^{N^2\\ell^{-A}}.$$ Every rational subspace $W\\subseteq\\mathbb R^m$ satisfying $H(W)\\le\\exp(N\\ell^D)$ has $$\\min_{U\\in\\mathcal C_{m,N}}d(U,W)\\le N\\ell^{-A}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-typical-ranks",
        "exact_quote": "For every pair of fixed constants $D,A>0$, there is a function $f_{D,A}(N)\\to\\infty$ such that the following holds for every $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$ and all sufficiently large $N$, with probability at least $1-2^{-Nf_{D,A}(N)}$ for $B=\\mathbf B_m$. Simultaneously for every rational subspace $W\\subseteq\\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\\le\\exp(N\\ell^D)$, one has*\n\n1.  *$\\dim W^\\perp-\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}(B|_{W^\\perp})\\le N\\ell^{-A}$;*\n\n2.  *for every coordinate set $X\\subseteq[m]$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}\\bigl[\\pi_{W^\\perp}Be_i\\bigr]_{i\\in X}\n      \\ge \\min\\{|X|,m-\\dim W\\}-N\\ell^{-A}.$$*\n\n*The ranks and the orthogonal projections in these conclusions are over $\\mathbb R$; no nonsingularity assumption on $B$ is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-inverse-rank",
        "exact_quote": "For every pair of fixed constants $D,A>0$, typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, simultaneously for every rational $W\\subseteq\\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\\le\\exp(N\\ell^D)$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}(B^{-1}|_W)\\ge\\dim W-N\\ell^{-A}.$$ Here $B^{-1}|_W$ is the restriction of the bilinear form with matrix $B^{-1}$, rather than the restriction of its associated linear map.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-corank",
        "exact_quote": "Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, one has, simultaneously for every prime $p$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{corank}}_{\\mathbb F_p}(B\\bmod p)\\le N^{3/5}.$$ More precisely, uniformly for $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the probability of nonsingularity together with failure of this assertion is at most $2^{-\\Omega(N^{6/5})}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-corank",
          "exact_quote": "Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, one has, simultaneously for every prime $p$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{corank}}_{\\mathbb F_p}(B\\bmod p)\\le N^{3/5}.$$ More precisely, uniformly for $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the probability of nonsingularity together with failure of this assertion is at most $2^{-\\Omega(N^{6/5})}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, one has, simultaneously for every prime $p$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{corank}}_{\\mathbb F_p}(B\\bmod p)\\le N^{3/5}.$$ More precisely, uniformly for $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the probability of nonsingularity together with failure of this assertion is at most $2^{-\\Omega(N^{6/5})}$.",
      "explanation": "Only primes dividing the nonzero determinant can have positive corank. Combining the determinant bound with the field-uniform corank tail controls every prime simultaneously.",

        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $A>0$, there is a fixed $C_A>0$ and a function $f_A(N)\\to\\infty$ such that, for every $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$ and all sufficiently large $N$, with probability at least $1-2^{-Nf_A(N)}$, the number of eigenvalues of $B=\\mathbf B_m$ in $$[-\\sqrt N\\,\\ell^{-C_A},\\ \\sqrt N\\,\\ell^{-C_A}]$$ is at most $N\\ell^{-A}$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-discriminant-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite abelian $p$-group with a perfect symmetric pairing $\\lambda:G\\times G\\to\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z$. It is an orthogonal direct sum of blocks of the following forms:*\n\n1.  *a cyclic group of order $p^j$ whose generator has self-pairing $a/p^j\\bmod\\mathbb Z$, with $p\\nmid a$;*\n\n2.  *when $p=2$, a group $(\\mathbb Z/2^j\\mathbb Z)^2$ with pairing matrix $$2^{-j}\\begin{pmatrix}2a&c\\\\c&2d\\end{pmatrix}\\bmod\\mathbb Z,\n      \\qquad c\\ \\text{odd}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-discriminant-blocks",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite abelian $p$-group with a perfect symmetric pairing $\\lambda:G\\times G\\to\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z$.           "source_id": "PM-239.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-primary-coloring",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:discriminant-blocks\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:discriminant-blocks\">22</a>, put $h=b_3$, with zero exponents appended as necessary. In an orthogonal block decomposition, let $U$ be the sum of the blocks of exponent strictly greater than $h$, and let $T$ be the remaining sum. There is a coloring of the isotropic elements of $G$ with $C_p$ colors such that elements $x,y$ of one color satisfy $$\\label{eq:primary-color-pairing}\n p^h\\lambda_U(x_U,y_U)=0\\quad\\text{in }\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z.$$ Here $x_U,y_U$ denote the projections to $U$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-colored-image-order",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a set of isotropic elements of $G_B$ in a single overall color, and let $$H=\\langle x-y:x,y\\in X\\rangle\\subset G_B.$$ For the restricted pairing map $$\\theta_H:H\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(H,\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z),\\qquad\n \\theta_H(u)=\\lambda_B(u,\\cdot)|_H,$$ one has $$|\\mathop{\\mathrm{im}}\\theta_H|\\le N^{NJ_B}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pairing-determinant",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a lattice, let $V=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}L$, and let $q$ be a symmetric bilinear form on $V$ such that $q(L,L)\\subset\\mathbb Q$. Write $$K=L\\cap\\operatorname{rad}q,\\qquad\n S=V\\cap(\\operatorname{rad}q)^\\perp,\\qquad\n \\bar L=\\operatorname{proj}_S L.$$ Then $K$ spans the radical and is primitive in $L$, and $\\bar L$ is a lattice in $S$. If $t$ is the order of the image of $$L\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(L,\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z),\\qquad\n x\\longmapsto q(x,\\cdot)\\bmod\\mathbb Z,$$ then $$\\label{eq:pairing-determinant}\n \\left|\\det_{\\mathrm{orth}}(q|_S)\\right|\n \\ge \\frac{1}{t(\\det\\bar L)^2}\n =\\frac{(\\det K)^2}{t(\\det L)^2}.$$ Here the determinant on the left is taken in an orthonormal basis of $S$, and all rank-zero determinants are one.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gated-deletion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a nonsingular symmetric $r\\times r$ matrix with entries in $\\{0,1\\}$, where $r\\ge1$. Either a principal submatrix obtained by deleting one coordinate is nonsingular, or there are distinct coordinates $i,j$ such that, with $I=[r]\\setminus\\{i,j\\}$, $$C=B_{I,I}\\quad\\hbox{is nonsingular},\\qquad\n v_1=B_{I,i}\\in\\mathcal S(C),\\quad v_2=B_{I,j}\\in\\mathcal S(C).$$ For the empty minor we use determinant one, and $\\mathcal S$ contains its unique empty column.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gated-deletion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a nonsingular symmetric $r\\times r$ matrix with entries in $\\{0,1\\}$, where $r\\ge1$.           "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $B$ be a nonsingular symmetric $r\\times r$ matrix with entries in $\\{0,1\\}$, where $r\\ge1$.       "explanation": "A nonzero inverse diagonal entry permits deletion of a coordinate. Otherwise a largest inverse off-diagonal entry permits deletion of a pair, and both removed cross columns satisfy admissibility for the remaining minor.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-potential-step",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $A>0$, the following holds typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$. For each $h\\in\\{1,2\\}$ with $m+h\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the random extension in Equation <a href=\"#eq:random-extension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:random-extension\">[eq:random-extension]</a> satisfies $$\\label{eq:potential-step}\n \\mathbb E\\!\\left[\n   \\mathbf 1_{\\{\\det B\\ne0\\}}\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal G_h}\\,2^{N\\Psi(B)}\n   \\,\\middle|\\, C\\right]\n \\le 2^{N(\\Psi(C)+\\ell^{-A})}.$$ The typical condition is imposed only on the starting matrix $C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-potential-step",

          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every fixed $A>0$, the following holds typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-layer-quotient",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite abelian group, let $v\\in G$, and put $H=G/\\langle v\\rangle$. Fix a prime $p$, and define $u_t$ by $$p^{u_t}=|\\langle v+p^tG\\rangle|\\quad\\hbox{in }G/p^tG\n \\qquad(t\\ge0).$$ Then $u_0=0$, $u_t-u_{t-1}\\in\\{0,1\\}$ for $t\\ge1$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n F_H(p,t)&=F_G(p,t)-u_t,\\label{eq:quotient-prefix}\\\\\n k_H(p,e)&=k_G(p,e)-(u_e-u_{e-1})\\in\n           \\{k_G(p,e),k_G(p,e)-1\\}.\\label{eq:layer-interlacing}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Call a positive layer $(p,e)$ of $G$ *retained* if $k_H(p,e)=k_G(p,e)$. On every maximal interval of consecutive layers where $k_G(p,e)$ has a fixed positive value, the retained layers form an initial interval, possibly empty. We call such an interval of constant height a *plateau*.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-retention-lattice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a nonsingular binary matrix of size $m$, and let $\\pi:\\mathbb Z^m\\twoheadrightarrow H$ be a quotient map to a finite abelian group such that $C\\mathbb Z^m\\subseteq\\ker\\pi$. Let $E$ be any set of positive layers of $H$. There is a lattice $K_E$, depending only on $\\pi,H,E$, with $$\\label{eq:retention-lattice}\n C\\mathbb Z^m\\subseteq K_E\\subseteq\\mathbb Z^m,\\qquad\n [\\mathbb Z^m:K_E]=N^{Nq(E)},\\qquad\n q(E)=\\sum_{(p,e)\\in E} k_H(p,e)\\nu_p,$$ such that the following implication holds for every $v\\in\\mathbb Z^m$: if every layer in $E$ is retained in $H\\longrightarrow H/\\langle\\pi(v)\\rangle$, then $v\\in K_E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cube-concentration",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $A>0$, typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, one has simultaneously for every lattice $B\\mathbb Z^m\\subseteq K\\subseteq\\mathbb Z^m$ $$\\label{eq:cube-concentration}\n M_K\\le 2^{N(1-c(q_K)+\\ell^{-A})}.$$ The typical event may depend on $A$, but it does not depend on $K$.         "exact_quote": "Fix $C,C'>0$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-witness-pricing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $D_0>0$, and put $\\eta=\\ell^{-D_0}$ and $\\rho=\\eta^3$. Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the following assertion holds simultaneously for every $B\\mathbb Z^m\\subseteq K\\subseteq\\mathbb Z^m$.*\n\n\n*Let $S_1,\\ldots,S_h\\subset\\{0,1\\}^m$ be nonempty subsets, each $\\rho$-robust on its affine span, where $h\\le3/\\eta$. Suppose $S_i-S_i\\subset K$, and put $W_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}(S_i-S_i)$. Suppose also that $W=\\sum_i W_i$ is a direct sum. Write $$\\dim W_i=t_iN,\\qquad t=\\sum_i t_i,\\qquad |S_i|=2^{\\alpha_iN}.$$ If $D\\subset[m]$ has $|D|=xN$, $x\\le t$, and $M_K(D)\\le2^{\\eta N}$, then $$\\label{eq:witness-pricing}\n xt-\\frac{x^2}{2}\n \\le \\sum_i t_i(t_i-\\alpha_i)+\\eta t+\\eta^3.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-witness-pricing",

Suppose $S_i-S_i\\subset K$, and put $W_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}(S_i-S_i)$. Suppose also that $W=\\sum_i W_i$ is a direct sum. Write $$\\dim W_i=t_iN,\\qquad t=\\sum_i t_i,\\qquad |S_i|=2^{\\alpha_iN}.$$ If $D\\subset[m]$ has $|D|=xN$, $x\\le t$, and $M_K(D)\\le2^{\\eta N}$, then $$\\label{eq:witness-pricing}\n xt-\\frac{x^2}{2}\n \\le \\sum_i t_i(t_i-\\alpha_i)+\\eta t+\\eta^3.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-certificate-pricing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $A>0$ and at most two positive constants $w_i$. Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the following assertion holds. The typical set is obtained by imposing Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cube-concentration\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cube-concentration\">31</a> at a sufficiently large fixed precision depending on $A$ and these constants.*\n\n*Let $\\mathcal F$ denote exposed information which fixes $C$, a quotient map $G_C\\twoheadrightarrow G$, and the data described next. Suppose that, conditionally on $\\mathcal F$, a tested column $v$ is uniform on $\\{0,1\\}^m$. Partition the layers of $G$ into at most two categories, each a union of whole plateaus. In category $i$, assume that every height is at least $w_i$; only $w_i=1,2$ will be needed. The partition is part of the exposed data. In the quotient of $G$ by the image of $v$, an outcome may mark some of the retained layers. Require that the marked layers on each plateau form a prefix. The marks may also depend on other random bits in the experiment.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal A$ be any subevent of this experiment. There is a partition of $\\mathcal A$ into at most $2^{N\\ell^{-A}}$ classes, constructed before choosing a representative or any target weights, with the following property. Take any nonempty class $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$, any representative outcome in it, and write $s_i$ and $u_i$ for the representative’s sums of the weights $\\nu_p$ of the marked and unmarked layers in category $i$. For any choices $0\\le x_i\\le s_i$, $$\\label{eq:certificate-pricing}\n \\Pr(\\mathcal A_\\alpha\\mid\\mathcal F)\n \\le 2^{-N\\left(\n c\\left(\\sum_i w_i x_i\\right)\n       -\\sum_i\\mathcal E(s_i,u_i,x_i)-\\ell^{-A}\\right)}.$$ Here $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$ already includes the restriction to $\\mathcal A$; the probability is conditioned only on the exposed information. The class count and the estimate are uniform in that information, the quotient, the subevent, and the representative. In particular, the exposed information may include a preceding column that fixes $G$, provided the tested column remains conditionally uniform.",
        "source_id": "PM-239.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-certificate-pricing",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $A>0$ and at most two positive constants $w_i$. Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the following assertion holds. The typical set is obtained by imposing Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cube-concentration\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cube-concentration\">31</a> at a sufficiently large fixed precision depending on $A$ and these constants.*\n\n*Let $\\mathcal F$ denote exposed information which fixes $C$, a quotient map $G_C\\twoheadrightarrow G$, and the data described next. Suppose that, conditionally on $\\mathcal F$, a tested column $v$ is uniform on $\\{0,1\\}^m$. Partition the layers of $G$ into at most two categories, each a union of whole plateaus. In category $i$, assume that every height is at least $w_i$; only $w_i=1,2$ will be needed. The partition is part of the exposed data. In the quotient of $G$ by the image of $v$, an outcome may mark some of the retained layers. Require that the marked layers on each plateau form a prefix. The marks may also depend on other random bits in the experiment.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal A$ be any subevent of this experiment. There is a partition of $\\mathcal A$ into at most $2^{N\\ell^{-A}}$ classes, constructed before choosing a representative or any target weights, with the following property. Take any nonempty class $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$, any representative outcome in it, and write $s_i$ and $u_i$ for the representative’s sums of the weights $\\nu_p$ of the marked and unmarked layers in category $i$. For any choices $0\\le x_i\\le s_i$, $$\\label{eq:certificate-pricing}\n \\Pr(\\mathcal A_\\alpha\\mid\\mathcal F)\n \\le 2^{-N\\left(\n c\\left(\\sum_i w_i x_i\\right)\n       -\\sum_i\\mathcal E(s_i,u_i,x_i)-\\ell^{-A}\\right)}.$$ Here $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$ already includes the restriction to $\\mathcal A$; the probability is conditioned only on the exposed information. The class count and the estimate are uniform in that information, the quotient, the subevent, and the representative. In particular, the exposed information may include a preceding column that fixes $G$, provided the tested column remains conditionally uniform.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $A>0$ and at most two positive constants $w_i$. Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the following assertion holds. The typical set is obtained by imposing Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cube-concentration\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cube-concentration\">31</a> at a sufficiently large fixed precision depending on $A$ and these constants.*\n\n*Let $\\mathcal F$ denote exposed information which fixes $C$, a quotient map $G_C\\twoheadrightarrow G$, and the data described next. Suppose that, conditionally on $\\mathcal F$, a tested column $v$ is uniform on $\\{0,1\\}^m$. Partition the layers of $G$ into at most two categories, each a union of whole plateaus. In category $i$, assume that every height is at least $w_i$; only $w_i=1,2$ will be needed. The partition is part of the exposed data. In the quotient of $G$ by the image of $v$, an outcome may mark some of the retained layers. Require that the marked layers on each plateau form a prefix. The marks may also depend on other random bits in the experiment.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal A$ be any subevent of this experiment. There is a partition of $\\mathcal A$ into at most $2^{N\\ell^{-A}}$ classes, constructed before choosing a representative or any target weights, with the following property. Take any nonempty class $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$, any representative outcome in it, and write $s_i$ and $u_i$ for the representative’s sums of the weights $\\nu_p$ of the marked and unmarked layers in category $i$. For any choices $0\\le x_i\\le s_i$, $$\\label{eq:certificate-pricing}\n \\Pr(\\mathcal A_\\alpha\\mid\\mathcal F)\n \\le 2^{-N\\left(\n c\\left(\\sum_i w_i x_i\\right)\n       -\\sum_i\\mathcal E(s_i,u_i,x_i)-\\ell^{-A}\\right)}.$$ Here $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$ already includes the restriction to $\\mathcal A$; the probability is conditioned only on the exposed information. The class count and the estimate are uniform in that information, the quotient, the subevent, and the representative. In particular, the exposed information may include a preceding column that fixes $G$, provided the tested column remains conditionally uniform.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Symmetric Bernoulli sign ensemble** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $A_n$ be a symmetric $n\times n$ random matrix whose entries on and above the diagonal are independent and uniform on $\{-1,1\}$.
- **Binary matrix ensemble** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A *binary matrix* of size $m$ is a symmetric $m\times m$ matrix whose entries on and above the diagonal are independent and uniform on $\{0,1\}$.
- **Reference scale and dimension window** ([source](#pm-h-4)): $$ The resulting first row and column consist of ones. Conditional on the original first row, the lower principal block still has independent uniform signs on and above its diagonal: every one of these signs has only been multiplied by a sign determined by the first row. Its Schur complement at the top left entry is therefore $-2\mathbf B_{n-1}$. The two operations and the Schur complement preserve singularity. ◻ </details> For the remainder of the proof, $N$ is a reference integer tending to infinity and $$\ell=\log\log N,\qquad \mathcal I_N=\{m\in\mathbb Z:N-N^{7/10}\le m\le N\}.$$
- **Typical property** ([source](#pm-h-4)): We say that a property of $\mathbf B_m$ *holds typically* if its failure probability is at most $2^{-Nf(N)}$, uniformly for $m\in\mathcal I_N$, for some $f(N)\to\infty$.
- **Admissible columns** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a nonsingular binary matrix $B$ of size $m$, define $$\label{eq:admissible-set} \mathcal S(B)= \left\{z\in\{0,1\}^m: z^{\mathsf T}B^{-1}z\in\{0,1\},\quad \\|B^{-1}z\\|_\infty\le1\right\}.$$
- **Discriminant group** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For nonsingular $B$, the finite abelian group $$G_B=\mathbb Z^m/B\mathbb Z^m$$ has order $\|\det B\|$.
- **Primary invariant exponents** ([source](#eq:group-order)): For a finite abelian group $G$, write its $p$-primary part as $$G_p\cong\bigoplus_{i\ge1}\mathbb Z/p^{b_{p,i}}\mathbb Z,\qquad b_{p,1}\ge b_{p,2}\ge\cdots\ge0,$$ where only finitely many exponents are positive.
- **Layer height and weight** ([source](#eq:group-order)): For $e\ge1$, put $$k_G(p,e)=\#\{i:b_{p,i}\ge e\},\qquad \nu_p=\frac{\log_N p}{N}.$$ We call $(p,e)$ with $k_G(p,e)>0$ a *layer*, its integer $k_G(p,e)$ its *height*, and $\nu_p$ its *weight*.
- **Arithmetic potential** ([source](#eq:potential)): Write $k_B(p,e)=k_{G_B}(p,e)$, set $\beta=7/5$, and define $$\label{eq:potential} \Psi(B)=\sum_{p,e}\nu_p\,\phi(k_B(p,e)),\qquad \phi(0)=\phi(1)=0,\quad \phi(2)=\beta,\quad \phi(k)=2k\quad(k\ge3).$$
- **Lattice determinant** ([source](#pm-h-8)): If $b_1,\ldots,b_r$ is a basis of $L$, its *determinant* is the Euclidean covolume $$\det L=\det\bigl(\langle b_i,b_j\rangle_{i,j=1}^r\bigr)^{1/2}.$$
- **Primitive sublattice** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We call $M$ *primitive*, or *saturated*, when $M=\overline M$.
- **Dual lattice in its span** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The dual lattice is always taken in the span: $$L^*=\{y\in E:\langle y,x\rangle\in\mathbb Z\text{ for all }x\in L\}.$$
- **Rational subspace height** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Its integer lattice $\Lambda_W=W\cap\mathbb Z^m$ has rank $\dim W$, and its *height* is $$H(W)=\det\Lambda_W.$$
- **Lattice Gaussian mass** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a lattice $L$ and $s>0$, define its Gaussian mass by $$\rho_s(L)=\sum_{x\in L}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\pi\\|x\\|^2}{s^2}\right).$$
- **Robust finite affine set** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For $\rho>0$, call $\mathcal A$ *$\rho$-robust* if every affine subspace $E\subset F$ of codimension $t$ satisfies $$\|\mathcal A\cap E\|\le 2^{-\rho t}\|\mathcal A\|.$$
- **Rank distance** ([source](#sec:low-height)): For rational subspaces $U,V\subseteq\mathbb R^m$, define their *rank distance* by $$d(U,V)=\dim U+\dim V-2\dim(U\cap V).$$
- **Restricted bilinear form** ([source](#sec:typical)): For a real symmetric matrix $B$ and a subspace $U\subseteq\mathbb R^m$, write $B\|_U$ for the bilinear form $(u,v)\mapsto u^{\mathsf T}Bv$ restricted to $U\times U$.
- **Discriminant pairing** ([source](#pm-h-17)): For a nonsingular symmetric integer matrix $B$, the formula $$\lambda_B(\bar x,\bar y)=x^{\mathsf T}B^{-1}y\bmod\mathbb Z \qquad(\bar x,\bar y\in G_B)$$ defines a symmetric pairing with values in $\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$.
- **Isotropic element** ([source](#pm-h-17)): An element is *isotropic* if its self-pairing is zero.
- **Layer prefix** ([source](#pm-h-20)): For a finite abelian group $G$, a prime $p$, and an integer $t\ge0$, define $$\label{eq:layer-prefix} F_G(p,t)=\sum_{e=1}^{t}k_G(p,e) =\sum_i\min\{b_{p,i},t\} =\log_p\|G/p^tG\|.$$
- **Retained layer** ([source](#stmt-lem-layer-quotient)): Call a positive layer $(p,e)$ of $G$ *retained* if $k_H(p,e)=k_G(p,e)$.
- **Constant-height plateau** ([source](#stmt-lem-layer-quotient)): We call such an interval of constant height a *plateau*.
- **Cube concentration profile** ([source](#eq:cube-profile)): For $0\le d\le1$, write $f=1-d$ and define $$\label{eq:cube-profile} \begin{split} c(d/2)&=1-fz(d/f),\\ z(u)&= \begin{cases} (1+\sqrt{2u-u^2})^{-1},&0\le u\le1,\\ 1/2,&u\ge1. \end{cases} \end{split}$$
- **Certificate entropy cost** ([source](#eq:entropy-cost)): For $s,u\ge0$ and $0\le x\le s$, define $$\label{eq:entropy-cost} \mathcal E(s,u,x) =(s+u)H_2\!\left(\frac{x}{s+u}\right) -sH_2\!\left(\frac{x}{s}\right),$$ using the continuous values when a denominator vanishes.
- **High-layer tail** ([source](#sec:minor-paths)): For a nonsingular matrix $B$ and an integer $s\ge0$, define $$W_s(B)=\sum_{p,e}(k_B(p,e)-s)_+\nu_p, \qquad x_+=\max\{x,0\}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-sharp-singularity-rate | \Pr(\det A_n=0) | = | $2^{-n+o(n)}$ | As the matrix dimension tends to infinity. |
| q-finite-corank-tail | \Pr\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb K}\mathbf B_m\ge k\bigr) | ≤ | $\sum_{j=k}^{m}\binom mj\,2^{-j(j+1)/2}$ | The stated finite corank range. |
| q-corank-one-column-factor | \Pr(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}\mathbf B_N=1) | ≤ | $N\,2^{-N}\,\mathbb E\|\mathcal S(\mathbf B_{N-1})\|$ | The binary dimension in the column-reduction statement. |
| q-height-two-potential-coefficient | \beta | = | $7/5$ | Fixed coefficient independent of the reference dimension. |
| q-potential-upper-bound | \Psi(B) | ≤ | $1$ | The singular-matrix indicator convention also assigns the defined value. |
| q-static-admissible-count | \|\mathcal S(B)\| | ≤ | $2^{N(\Psi(B)+\ell^{-A})}$ | Precision is fixed before the reference dimension tends to infinity. |
| q-potential-moment | \mathbb E\!\left[\mathbf 1_{\{\det\mathbf B_{N-1}\ne0\}}\,2^{N\Psi(\mathbf B_{N-1})}\right] | ≤ | $2^{o(N)}$ | As the reference dimension tends to infinity. |
| q-reverse-minkowski-mass | \rho_{T/\tau_j}(L) | ≤ | $\frac32$ | Positive rank and positive determinant threshold; the separate rank-zero convention is stated. |
| q-determinant-minimizer-rank | \mathop{\mathrm{rank}}L_0 | ≤ | $\frac{\Delta}{\log(T_0/T)}$ | The stronger threshold exceeds the minimizing threshold. |
| q-low-height-cover-size | \|\mathcal C_{m,N}\| | ≤ | $2^{N^2\ell^{-A}}$ | Height and precision exponents are fixed before the reference dimension tends to infinity. |
| q-low-height-cover-distance | \min_{U\in\mathcal C_{m,N}}d(U,W) | ≤ | $N\ell^{-A}$ | All stated ambient dimensions for sufficiently large reference dimension. |
| q-prime-corank-cap | \mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb F_p}(B\bmod p) | ≤ | $N^{3/5}$ | Uniform in matrix size throughout the terminal window. |
| q-small-spectrum-count | number of eigenvalues in the stated interval | ≤ | $N\ell^{-A}$ | The spectral interval and its fixed precision coefficient are those of the proposition; nonsingularity is unnecessary. |
| q-odd-primary-color-count | C_p | ≤ | $2^{b_2-b_3}$ | Odd primes and the invariant-exponent cutoff specified in the coloring lemma. |
| q-pairing-image-order | \|\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\theta_H\| | ≤ | $N^{NJ_B}$ | The high-layer weight of the same matrix. |
| q-retention-lattice-index | [\mathbb Z^m:K_E] | = | $N^{Nq(E)}$ | The lattice is fixed by the exposed quotient map and the selected layer set. |
| q-cube-coset-concentration | M_K | ≤ | $2^{N(1-c(q_K)+\ell^{-A})}$ | Simultaneous in the lattice, including choices depending on the matrix. |
| q-retention-weight-probability | conditional probability of retained weight at least the stated threshold | ≤ | $2^{-N\sigma/2}$ | The threshold is the retention scale defined in the paper; destination nonsingularity is unnecessary. |
| q-heavy-retention-probability | \Pr\{Q\in[q,2q)\mid\mathcal F\} | ≤ | $2^{-3qN/5}$ | The lower endpoint and heavy-height cutoff are those specified in the lemma. |

The quantity $$\\frac{\\det M}{T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}},\\qquad M\\subset L\\text{ a sublattice},$$ has a minimum, attained by a primitive sublattice $L_0$.         }

There is a coloring of the isotropic elements of $G$ with $C_p$ colors such that elements $x,y$ of one color satisfy $$\\label{eq:primary-color-pairing}\n p^h\\lambda_U(x_U,y_U)=0\\quad\\text{in }\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z.$$ Here $x_U,y_U$ denote the projections to $U$. The color counts may be chosen to satisfy $$C_p\\le 1+8b_1,\\qquad\n C_p\\le 2^{b_2-b_3}\\quad\\text{if \\(p\\) is odd}.$$",
