<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-sign-bit** [claim](#stmt-lem-sign-bit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sign-bit): Diagonal congruence followed by a Schur complement. -> Exact singularity-probability equivalence with the binary model.
- **step-principal-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-principal-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-principal-rank): Factor the symmetric matrix through a column-space basis and select independent rows. -> A full-rank nonsingular principal submatrix.
- **step-corank-tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-corank-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-corank-tail): Expose columns of a nonsingular principal block and count forced complementary entries. -> Field-uniform corank tail.
- **step-corank-one** [claim](#stmt-lem-corank-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-corank-reduction): Flip diagonal coordinates independent on the kernel and bound the number of preimages. -> Reduction of singularity to corank one.
- **step-column-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-column-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-column-reduction): Delete a largest kernel coordinate and use the adjugate and Schur complement. -> Corank-one probability bounded by expected admissible-column count.
- **step-lattice-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-lattice-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lattice-quotient): Extend a primitive basis and factor Euclidean volume under orthogonal projection. -> Quotient, dual, and preimage determinant identities.
- **step-height-submodularity** [claim](#stmt-lem-height-submodularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-height-submodularity): Project off the intersection, compare volumes, saturate, and use integer exterior vectors. -> Height submodularity and bounded-height finiteness.
- **step-normal-height** [claim](#stmt-lem-normal-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normal-height): Apply dual projection identities and factor determinants along primitive normal lattices. -> Normal-lattice height ratios and sublattice determinant bounds.
- **step-reverse-minkowski** [claim](#stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski): Rescale the lattice and invoke the cited Reverse Minkowski theorem in its span. -> Gaussian mass bound at the stated scale.
- **step-gaussian-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-count): Compare Gaussian weights of short points and apply Gaussian Poisson summation. -> Ball point counts and scale comparison.
- **step-determinant-minimizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-det-minimizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-det-minimizer): Use finiteness of bounded exterior norms, saturate a minimizer, and compare lifted quotients. -> Primitive determinant minimizer and uniform quotient lower bounds.
- **step-robust-extraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-robust-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-robust-extraction): Maximize size penalized by affine dimension over the finite family of restrictions. -> Robust affine restriction and independent-tuple multiplicity.
- **step-robust-height** [claim](#stmt-lem-robust-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-robust-height): Remove a determinant minimizer, count quotient projections, and apply robustness to a largest fiber. -> Controlled image-lattice determinant and difference-space height.
- **step-low-height-cover** [claim](#stmt-thm-low-height-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-low-height-cover): Build greatest minimizing spaces along coordinate equations and encode normal flags with a height-budget bound on expensive choices. -> A fixed rank-distance cover of all eligible rational subspaces.
- **step-typical-ranks** [claim](#stmt-prop-typical-ranks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-typical-ranks): Reduce primitive integer bases modulo two, union over a fixed cover, and transfer deficiencies by rank-distance stability. -> Simultaneous real rank tests for all eligible spaces and coordinate sets.
- **step-inverse-rank** [claim](#stmt-cor-inverse-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-inverse-rank): Identify the two restricted-form radicals through multiplication by the nonsingular matrix. -> Uniform inverse-form restriction rank.
- **step-prime-corank** [claim](#stmt-prop-prime-corank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-prime-corank): Limit relevant primes using the determinant bound and union the field-uniform corank tails. -> Corank cap simultaneously over every prime.
- **step-small-spectrum** [claim](#stmt-prop-typical-spectrum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-typical-spectrum): Enumerate closed-walk moments, majorize a spectral tent, concentrate convex traces, and use rank-one interlacing. -> Typical eigenvalue count near zero.
- **step-primary-blocks** [claim](#stmt-lem-discriminant-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-discriminant-blocks): Split a maximal-denominator cyclic block or an even-primary paired block and iterate orthogonal complements. -> Orthogonal block decomposition.
- **step-primary-coloring** [claim](#stmt-lem-primary-coloring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-primary-coloring): Separate deep elements and color the remaining top-block coordinates by valuations, pivots, and modular ratios. -> Colors with controlled top-block mutual denominators.
- **step-colored-image-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-colored-image-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-colored-image-order): Bound the pairing image separately on top and remaining primary blocks, then multiply over primes. -> Pairing image controlled by high-layer weight.
- **step-pairing-determinant** [claim](#stmt-lem-pairing-determinant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pairing-determinant): Remove the rational radical and use an integral pairing-kernel basis on one side of the rational form matrix. -> Orthogonal-form determinant lower bound.
- **step-static-admissible-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-admissible-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pairing-determinant-2): Extract a robust largest color, embed its differences in an inverse graph lattice, and compare arithmetic determinant lower bounds with spectral upper bounds. -> Admissible-column estimate in terms of arithmetic potential.
- **step-gated-deletion** [claim](#stmt-lem-gated-deletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gated-deletion): Inspect inverse diagonal entries; otherwise use a largest off-diagonal inverse entry, complementary minors, and block inversion. -> A nonsingular smaller principal minor with the required extension gate.
- **step-layer-quotient** [claim](#stmt-lem-layer-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-quotient): Compute the order of the element modulo successive prime powers and difference the quotient prefix orders. -> Layer interlacing and prefix retention on plateaus.
- **step-retention-lattice** [claim](#stmt-lem-retention-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-retention-lattice): Assign divisibility exponents by selected layers and pull the resulting subgroup back through the exposed quotient map. -> Necessary integer-lattice membership with exact index.
- **step-symmetric-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-layer-inequality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-inequality): Use character annihilators and symmetry to count compatible first and second block equations modulo prime powers. -> Destination layer-prefix inequality.
- **step-exact-basis-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-basis-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-basis-count): Encode pivots and successive rational graph functionals; count quotient images and low-dimensional cube fibers. -> Uniform ordered basis count.
- **step-witness-pricing** [claim](#stmt-prop-witness-pricing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-witness-pricing): Double-count matrices with robust basis lists, encode revealed columns and candidate lattices, and compare remaining-row savings with basis multiplicity. -> Simultaneous robust-witness pricing inequality.
- **step-cube-concentration** [claim](#stmt-prop-cube-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-witness-pricing-2): Extract robust projection fibers, construct a minimum-cardinality coordinate witness list, and apply witness pricing to the list and to a full-cube extremizer. -> Simultaneous cube-coset bound for every intermediate lattice.
- **step-certificate-pricing** [claim](#stmt-prop-certificate-pricing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-certificate-pricing): Partition prefix marks by prime ranges and weight cells, count offered and candidate certificates, and convert their ratio into entropy costs. -> Conditional probability bounds for classes fixed before target selection.
- **step-retention-cost** [claim](#stmt-prop-retention-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-retention-cost): Mark all retained layers, choose a small certificate target, and compare the concentration profile near zero with the entropy cost. -> Uniform cost for noticeable retained layer weight.
- **step-budget-optimization** [claim](#stmt-lem-budget-optimization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-budget-optimization-2): Pass to a supporting slope, compute the entropy dual minimum, and verify explicit multiplier choices across the slope ranges. -> Certificate targets paying for the potential increment.
- **step-potential-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-potential-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-budget-optimization): For width one price a maximal-potential representative in each certificate class; for width two pay the gate using independent admissible columns. -> Local conditional exponential-potential estimate.
- **step-tail-recursion** [claim](#stmt-lem-tail-recursion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tail-recursion): Stop the destination prefix at the tail cutoff, use common-quotient interlacing, and apply the symmetric kernel inequality. -> Shifted-tail recursion with retention charges.
- **step-heavy-retention** [claim](#stmt-lem-heavy-retention-cost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heavy-retention-cost): Bound fixed-mask atoms with a maximal dissociated coordinate subset and count heavy prefix masks separately in the prime ranges. -> Heavy-retention probability estimate without typicality.
- **step-path-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-path-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heavy-retention-cost-2): Cover by fixed gated principal paths, control high tails along the long prefix, and split the terminal window into low potential or forced costly steps using auxiliary-statistic descent. -> Subexponential nonsingular potential moment.
- **step-main-rate** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-heavy-retention-cost-3): Combine the static count and potential moment, use the corank and column reductions, match the binary zero-row lower bound, and transfer back to signs. -> Sharp leading exponential singularity rate for the symmetric sign ensemble.

The Euclidean realization $$L/K:=\\pi_{F^\\perp}(L)$$ is a lattice of rank $r-k$, canonically isomorphic to the abstract quotient, and $$\\det L=\\det K\\,\\det(L/K),\\qquad\n \\det L^*=(\\det L)^{-1}.$$ The dual identities are $$(L/K)^*=L^*\\cap F^\\perp,\\qquad\n K^*=\\pi_F(L^*),$$ where $\\pi_F$ is orthogonal projection onto $F$. For any sublattice $M\\subset L/K$, its full preimage $\\widetilde M=\\{x\\in L:\\pi_{F^\\perp}x\\in M\\}$ has rank $k+\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M$ and satisfies $$\\det\\widetilde M=\\det K\\,\\det M.$$ These assertions include $k=0$, $k=r$, and $r=0$, with all rank-zero determinants equal to one.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-lattice-quotient",
      "inputs": [
        "Primitive sublattice",
        "Lattice determinant conventions"
      ],
      "method": "Extend a primitive basis and factor Euclidean volume under orthogonal projection.",
      "output": "Quotient, dual, and preimage determinant identities.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-lattice-quotient"
    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-submodularity",
          "exact_quote": "For rational subspaces $U,V\\subset\\mathbb R^m$, $$H(U+V)H(U\\cap V)\\le H(U)H(V).$$ Consequently $h(U+V)+h(U\\cap V)\\le h(U)+h(V)$. Every rational subspace has height at least one, and, for fixed $m$ and $C<\\infty$, there are only finitely many rational subspaces $W\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\\le C$. These statements include rank-zero subspaces.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-height-submodularity",
      "inputs": [
        "Orthogonal quotient identities",
        "Rational integer lattices"
      ],
      "method": "Project off the intersection, compare volumes, saturate, and use integer exterior vectors.",
      "output": "Height submodularity and bounded-height finiteness.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-height-submodularity"
    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-height",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V\\subset U\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be rational subspaces, and put $\\Lambda=\\Lambda_U^*$. The primitive normal lattice $$\\mathcal N_U(V)=\\Lambda\\cap V^\\perp$$ has rank $\\dim U-\\dim V$, and $$\\pi_V(\\Lambda)=\\Lambda_V^*,\\qquad\n \\det\\mathcal N_U(V)=\\frac{H(V)}{H(U)}.$$ More generally, for any sublattice $M\\subset\\Lambda$, set $V_M=U\\cap(\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}M)^\\perp$. Then $V_M$ is rational and $$\\det M=[\\mathcal N_U(V_M):M]\\frac{H(V_M)}{H(U)}\n \\ge \\frac1{H(U)}.$$ For $V=U$ the normal lattice has rank zero and determinant one; for $V=\\{0\\}$ it is $\\Lambda_U^*$. If $M=\\{0\\}$, the displayed index is one and the determinant formula gives one.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Normal-lattice height ratios and sublattice determinant bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-normal-height"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a lattice of rank $j\\ge1$, and let $T>0$. Suppose that $$\\det M\\ge T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}\\qquad\\text{for every sublattice }M\\subset L.$$ With $\\tau_j=10(\\log j+2)$, one has $$\\rho_{T/\\tau_j}(L)\\le\\frac32.$$ For a rank-zero lattice the Gaussian mass is one at every scale, so the corresponding assertion is immediate.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-reverse-minkowski",

      "method": "Rescale the lattice and invoke the cited Reverse Minkowski theorem in its span.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-count",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a rank-$j$ lattice. For $R\\ge0$ and $s>0$, $$\\#\\{x\\in L:\\|x\\|\\le R\\}\n \\le \\exp(\\pi R^2/s^2)\\rho_s(L).$$ For $s'\\ge s>0$, $$\\rho_{s'}(L)\\le (s'/s)^j\\rho_s(L).$$ In particular, under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:reverse-minkowski\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:reverse-minkowski\">12</a>, if $s_0=T/\\tau_j$, then for every $u\\ge s_0$, $$\\#\\{x\\in L:\\|x\\|\\le R\\}\n \\le\\frac32\\left(\\frac{u}{s_0}\\right)^j\n       \\exp(\\pi R^2/u^2).$$ When $j=0$, the point count and every Gaussian mass are one; the first inequality is immediate and the second is equality.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-count"

    {

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-det-minimizer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a lattice and let $T>0$. The quantity $$\\frac{\\det M}{T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}},\\qquad M\\subset L\\text{ a sublattice},$$ has a minimum, attained by a primitive sublattice $L_0$. It satisfies $$\\det L_0\\le T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}L_0},\\qquad\n \\det M\\ge T^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}\\quad\\text{for every sublattice }M\\subset L/L_0.$$ If in addition $T_0>T$, $\\Delta\\ge0$, and $$\\det M\\ge e^{-\\Delta}T_0^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M}\n       \\quad\\text{for every sublattice }M\\subset L,$$ then $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}L_0\\le\\frac{\\Delta}{\\log(T_0/T)}.$$ For $L=\\{0\\}$ the unique choice is $L_0=\\{0\\}$, and every assertion holds with rank zero and determinant one.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-robust-extraction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\varnothing\\ne\\mathcal A\\subset\\{0,1\\}^m$, let $\\rho>0$, and put $d=\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{aff}}\\mathcal A$. There is an affine restriction $\\mathcal A_0=\\mathcal A\\cap F$, with $F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{aff}}\\mathcal A_0$ of dimension $r$, such that $\\mathcal A_0$ is $\\rho$-robust and $$\\log_2|\\mathcal A_0|\n \\ge \\log_2|\\mathcal A|-\\rho(d-r)\n \\ge \\log_2|\\mathcal A|-\\rho m,\n \\qquad\n \\log_2|\\mathcal A_0|\\le r.$$ For any fixed $a_0\\in\\mathcal A_0$, the number of ordered $r$-tuples $(a_1,\\ldots,a_r)\\in\\mathcal A_0^r$ for which $a_1-a_0,\\ldots,a_r-a_0$ are independent is at least $$|\\mathcal A_0|^r\\prod_{t=1}^r(1-2^{-\\rho t})\n \\ge \\bigl((1-2^{-\\rho})|\\mathcal A_0|\\bigr)^r.$$ When $r=0$, $\\mathcal A_0$ is a singleton and the number of empty ordered tuples is one.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-robust-height",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $D>0$ and $C_0>0$. For all sufficiently large $N$, put $\\rho=\\ell^{-D}$. Let $m\\le N$, let $\\varnothing\\ne\\mathcal A\\subset\\{0,1\\}^m$ be $\\rho$-robust on its affine span $F$ of dimension $r$, and let $\\Phi:F\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^d$ be an injective rational affine map, meaning that it maps rational points of $F$ to rational points of $\\mathbb R^d$. Suppose that $$\\|\\Phi(a)-\\Phi(b)\\|\\le C_0\\sqrt N\n       \\qquad(a,b\\in\\mathcal A).$$ For any $a_0\\in\\mathcal A$, the group $$L_\\Phi=\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal A}\\mathbb Z\\bigl(\\Phi(a)-\\Phi(a_0)\\bigr)$$ is a rank-$r$ lattice, independent of the choice of $a_0$, and $$\\det L_\\Phi\\le \\exp\\bigl(O_{D,C_0}(N\\ell)\\bigr).$$ In particular, with $W=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}(\\mathcal A-\\mathcal A)$, $$H(W)\\le \\exp\\bigl(O_D(N\\ell)\\bigr).$$ If $r=0$, both lattices in question have rank zero and determinant one.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Controlled image-lattice determinant and difference-space height.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-robust-height"
    },

          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a lattice, let $V=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}L$, and let $q$ be a symmetric bilinear form on $V$ such that $q(L,L)\\subset\\mathbb Q$. Write $$K=L\\cap\\operatorname{rad}q,\\qquad\n S=V\\cap(\\operatorname{rad}q)^\\perp,\\qquad\n \\bar L=\\operatorname{proj}_S L.$$ Then $K$ spans the radical and is primitive in $L$, and $\\bar L$ is a lattice in $S$. If $t$ is the order of the image of $$L\\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(L,\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z),\\qquad\n x\\longmapsto q(x,\\cdot)\\bmod\\mathbb Z,$$ then $$\\label{eq:pairing-determinant}\n \\left|\\det_{\\mathrm{orth}}(q|_S)\\right|\n \\ge \\frac{1}{t(\\det\\bar L)^2}\n =\\frac{(\\det K)^2}{t(\\det L)^2}.$$ Here the determinant on the left is taken in an orthonormal basis of $S$, and all rank-zero determinants are one.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-layer-quotient",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a finite abelian group, let $v\\in G$, and put $H=G/\\langle v\\rangle$. Fix a prime $p$, and define $u_t$ by $$p^{u_t}=|\\langle v+p^tG\\rangle|\\quad\\hbox{in }G/p^tG\n \\qquad(t\\ge0).$$ Then $u_0=0$, $u_t-u_{t-1}\\in\\{0,1\\}$ for $t\\ge1$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n F_H(p,t)&=F_G(p,t)-u_t,\\label{eq:quotient-prefix}\\\\\n k_H(p,e)&=k_G(p,e)-(u_e-u_{e-1})\\in\n           \\{k_G(p,e),k_G(p,e)-1\\}.\\label{eq:layer-interlacing}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Call a positive layer $(p,e)$ of $G$ *retained* if $k_H(p,e)=k_G(p,e)$. On every maximal interval of consecutive layers where $k_G(p,e)$ has a fixed positive value, the retained layers form an initial interval, possibly empty. We call such an interval of constant height a *plateau*.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-nonsingular-deletion**: Every nonsingular symmetric binary matrix admits a nonsingular principal minor obtained by deleting a coordinate or a pair of distinct coordinates.
- **invariant-admissible-pair**: When no deletion of a single coordinate leaves a nonsingular principal minor, a pair can be deleted so that both removed cross columns are admissible for the remaining minor.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-asymmetric-deletion**: Apply the deletion construction to an arbitrary binary matrix. -> The inverse identities used to obtain an admissible pair depend on symmetry.
- **anti-singular-start**: Apply the inverse-based deletion construction to a singular starting matrix. -> The inverse needed to select the deletion does not exist.
- **anti-quadratic-only-gate**: Accept paired deletion after checking only the quadratic condition on each cross column. -> A column may satisfy the quadratic condition while failing the inverse-image norm bound required for admissibility.
- **anti-finite-asymptotic-certificate**: Treat finite deletion checks as certification of the sharp asymptotic singularity probability. -> The deletion lemma is a deterministic ingredient; the asymptotic rate also requires the admissible-column and potential-moment estimates.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The gated-deletion lemma has a finite exact construction: inspect the diagonal of the inverse, and otherwise delete a pair attaining the largest absolute inverse entry. The implemented result is this deterministic lemma, rather than the asymptotic singularity theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with name and either matrix or max_dimension.
# PRECONDITIONS: Explicit matrices are nonempty, symmetric, binary and nonsingular; max_dimension is an integer from 1 through 3.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of deletion certificates, one per nonsingular matrix in deterministic enumeration order.
# TIME: Polynomial rational elimination per matrix; exhaustive mode enumerates exponentially many matrices.
# SPACE: Quadratic rational storage per matrix, plus the output certificate list.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and fractions; no floating-point comparisons.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception; singular matrices are omitted only in exhaustive mode.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def inverse(a):
    n = len(a)
    t = [[Fraction(a[i][j]) for j in range(n)] + [Fraction(i == j) for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]
    for c in range(n):
        p = next((r for r in range(c, n) if t[r][c]), None)
        if p is None:
            return None
        t[c], t[p] = t[p], t[c]
        pivot = t[c][c]
        t[c] = [x / pivot for x in t[c]]
        for r in range(n):
            if r != c:
                factor = t[r][c]
                t[r] = [x - factor * y for x, y in zip(t[r], t[c])]
    return [row[n:] for row in t]

def deletion(inv):
    n = len(inv)
    for i in range(n):
        if inv[i][i] != 0:
            return [i]
    i, j = max(((i, j) for i in range(n) for j in range(i + 1, n)), key=lambda pair: abs(inv[pair[0]][pair[1]]))
    return [i, j]

def validate(a):
    if not isinstance(a, list) or not a:
        raise ValueError('nonempty matrix required')
    n = len(a)
    if any(not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != n for row in a):
        raise ValueError('square matrix required')
    if any(type(x) is not int or x not in (0, 1) for row in a for x in row):
        raise ValueError('binary entries required')
    if any(a[i][j] != a[j][i] for i in range(n) for j in range(n)):
        raise ValueError('symmetry required')

def matrices(data):
    if 'matrix' in data:
        a = data['matrix']
        validate(a)
        inv = inverse(a)
        if inv is None:
            raise ValueError('nonsingular matrix required')
        yield a, inv
        return
    limit = data['max_dimension']
    if type(limit) is not int or not 1 <= limit <= 3:
        raise ValueError('invalid enumeration dimension')
    for n in range(1, limit + 1):
        positions = [(i, j) for i in range(n) for j in range(i, n)]
        for mask in range(1 << len(positions)):
            a = [[0] * n for _ in range(n)]
            for bit, (i, j) in enumerate(positions):
                a[i][j] = a[j][i] = (mask >> bit) & 1
            inv = inverse(a)
            if inv is not None:
                yield a, inv

data = json.load(sys.stdin)
certificates = []
for a, inv in matrices(data):
    removed = deletion(inv)
    certificates.append({'removed': removed})
print(json.dumps(certificates, separators=(',', ':')))
```

is int and 0 <= i < len(a) for i in removed)\n    assert len(set(removed)) == len(removed)\n    keep = [i for i in range(len(a)) if i not in removed]\n    c = [[a[i][j] for j in keep] for i in keep]\n    assert determinant(c) != 0\n    if len(removed) == 2:\n        for j in removed:\n            assert admissible(c, [a[i][j] for i in keep])\n\ntry:\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as int\n    inputs = domain(data)\n    assert len(inputs) == expected_count\n    assert isinstance(output, list) and len(output) == len(inputs)\n    for a, certificate in zip(inputs, output):\n The complementary principal minor must be nonsingular; for a pair, both cross columns must satisfy the exact admissibility conditions. The empty principal minor has determinant one.",
    "reason": "The gated-deletion lemma has a finite exact construction: inspect the diagonal of the inverse, and otherwise delete a pair attaining the largest absolute inverse entry. The implemented result is this deterministic lemma, rather than the asymptotic singularity theorem.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with name and either matrix or max_dimension.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Explicit matrices are nonempty, symmetric, binary and nonsingular; max_dimension is an integer from 1 through 3.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of deletion certificates, one per nonsingular matrix in deterministic enumeration order.\n# TIME: Polynomial rational elimination per matrix; exhaustive mode enumerates exponentially many matrices.\n# SPACE: Quadratic rational storage per matrix, plus the output certificate list.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and fractions; no floating-point comparisons.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception; singular matrices are omitted only in exhaustive mode.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef inverse(a):\n    n = len(a)\n    t = [[Fraction(a[i][j]) for j in range(n)] + [Fraction(i == j) for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]\n    for c in range(n):\n        p = next((r for r in range(c, n) if t[r][c]), None)\n        if p is None:\n            return None\n        t[c], t[p] = t[p], t[c]\n        pivot = t[c][c]\n        t[c] = [x / pivot for x in t[c]]\n        for r in range(n):\n            if r != c:\n                factor = t[r][c]\n                t[r] = [x - factor * y for x, y in zip(t[r], t[c])]\n    return [row[n:] for row in t]\n\ndef deletion(inv):\n    n = len(inv)\n    for i in range(n):\n        if inv[i][i] != 0:\n            return [i]\n    i, j = max(((i, j) for i in range(n) for j in range(i + 1, n)), key=lambda pair: abs(inv[pair[0]][pair[1]]))\n    return [i, j]\n\ndef validate(a):\n    if not isinstance(a, list) or not a:\n        raise ValueError('nonempty matrix required')\n    n = len(a)\n    if any(not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != n for row in a):\n        raise ValueError('square matrix required')\n    if any(type(x) is not int or x not in (0, 1) for row in a for x in row):\n        raise ValueError('binary entries required')\n    if any(a[i][j] != a[j][i] for i in range(n) for j in range(n)):\n        raise ValueError('symmetry required')\n\ndef matrices(data):\n    if 'matrix' in data:\n        a = data['matrix']\n        validate(a)\n        inv = inverse(a)\n        if inv is None:\n            raise ValueError('nonsingular matrix required')\n        yield a, inv\n        return\n    limit = data['max_dimension']\n    if type(limit) is not int or not 1 <= limit <= 3:\n        raise ValueError('invalid enumeration dimension')\n    for n in range(1, limit + 1):\n        positions = [(i, j) for i in range(n) for j in range(i, n)]\n        for mask in range(1 << len(positions)):\n            a = [[0] * n for _ in range(n)]\n            for bit, (i, j) in enumerate(positions):\n                a[i][j] = a[j][i] = (mask >> bit) & 1\n            inv = inverse(a)\n            if inv is not None:\n                yield a, inv\n\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\ncertificates = []\nfor a, inv in matrices(data):\n    removed = deletion(inv)\n    certificates.append({'removed': removed})\nprint(json.dumps(certificates, separators=(',', ':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The principal-rank proof supplies an exact construction for selecting a nonsingular principal submatrix while preserving symmetry. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The prescribed random matrix ensemble supports a singularity calculation, but the paper supplies no game dynamics, collision model, or simulation update construction. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The admissible-column definition gives a finite exact checking procedure for candidate bit columns of a supplied nonsingular binary matrix. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | Gated deletion provides a finite construction of a path of nonsingular principal minors, with an additional admissibility condition when a pair of coordinates is deleted. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | A finite abelian group's primary invariant exponents give a concrete finite representation of its layers and permit exact computation of quotient layer drops from a specified element. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sign-to-bit proof uses diagonal congruence and a Schur complement to preserve singularity; it supplies no accelerator circuit, arithmetic-width contract, or hardware performance construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lattice Gaussian-mass bound requires determinant control for every sublattice. It provides neither a cryptographic primitive nor a security reduction establishing that condition for a cryptographic instance. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The small-spectrum result concerns the specified binary random matrix ensemble and does not construct a training procedure or establish generalisation behavior for learned matrices. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scalar budget lemma proves the existence of certificate target weights under particular order budgets. It does not supply an optimisation procedure or an operations-research decision contract. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The retained-layer probability estimate depends on conditional uniformity of new columns after exposed information is fixed. It provides no distributed protocol or construction for preserving that assumption across communicating participants. |
| other (Finite abelian group arithmetic) | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The retention-lattice proof constructs an explicit divisibility subgroup from selected positive layers and takes its preimage under a supplied quotient map. |

          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-gated-deletion</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The cofactor identity gives $$\\det B_{[r]\\setminus\\{i\\},[r]\\setminus\\{i\\}}\n   =\\det B\\,(B^{-1})_{ii}.$$ Thus a nonzero diagonal entry of $B^{-1}$ gives the first alternative. Suppose instead that all diagonal entries of $B^{-1}$ vanish. Since $B^{-1}$ is nonsingular, it has a nonzero off-diagonal entry. Choose $i,j$ to maximize the absolute value of an entry of $B^{-1}$, and write $\\kappa=(B^{-1})_{ij}\\ne0$. Then $$(B^{-1})_{\\{i,j\\},\\{i,j\\}}=\n \\begin{pmatrix}0&\\kappa\\\\ \\kappa&0\\end{pmatrix}.$$ Jacobi’s complementary minor identity gives $$\\det C=\\det B\\,\n          \\det (B^{-1})_{\\{i,j\\},\\{i,j\\}}\n        =-\\kappa^2\\det B\\ne0.$$\n\nOrder the coordinates with $I$ first and write $$B=\\begin{pmatrix}C&V\\\\ V^{\\mathsf T}&D\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad V=(v_1\\ v_2),\\qquad\n T=D-V^{\\mathsf T}C^{-1}V.$$ Block inversion shows that $$T^{-1}=(B^{-1})_{\\{i,j\\},\\{i,j\\}}\n       =\\begin{pmatrix}0&\\kappa\\\\ \\kappa&0\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad\n (B^{-1})_{I,\\{i,j\\}}=-C^{-1}VT^{-1}.$$ The diagonal of $T$ is therefore zero. Its two diagonal equations give $v_a^{\\mathsf T}C^{-1}v_a=D_{aa}\\in\\{0,1\\}$ for $a=1,2$. Moreover, the two columns of the displayed inverse block are $-\\kappa C^{-1}v_2$ and $-\\kappa C^{-1}v_1$. Every entry in that block has absolute value at most $|\\kappa|$, by the choice of $i,j$. Hence $\\|C^{-1}v_a\\|_\\infty\\le1$ for $a=1,2$. These two facts are exactly the defining conditions for $v_1,v_2\\in\\mathcal S(C)$. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-lem-gated-deletion"
        }
      ],

Let $E$ be any set of positive layers of $H$. There is a lattice $K_E$, depending only on $\\pi,H,E$, with $$\\label{eq:retention-lattice}\n C\\mathbb Z^m\\subseteq K_E\\subseteq\\mathbb Z^m,\\qquad\n [\\mathbb Z^m:K_E]=N^{Nq(E)},\\qquad\n q(E)=\\sum_{(p,e)\\in E} k_H(p,e)\\nu_p,$$ such that the following implication holds for every $v\\in\\mathbb Z^m$: if every layer in $E$ is retained in $H\\longrightarrow H/\\langle\\pi(v)\\rangle$, then $v\\in K_E$.",

This holds in every primary coordinate, so $\\pi(v)\\in T_E$ and $v\\in K_E$, as required. ◻",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-prescribed-sign-ensemble** (hypothesis): The sharp singularity rate assumes symmetry and independent uniform signs on and above the diagonal. Blocked use: ['Transfer the rate to biased, sparse, asymmetric, or additionally correlated ensembles without another theorem.']
- **limit-asymptotic-rate** (approximation): The leading exponential rate does not specify an exact probability or a numerical error bound at a supplied finite dimension. Blocked use: ['Use the leading exponential expression as a finite confidence certificate.']
- **limit-sign-bit-transformation** (formal_scope): The sign-to-bit equivalence uses multiplication by a sign, diagonal congruence, and a Schur complement. Replacing signs by bits entrywise is not the stated transformation. Blocked use: ['Replace sign entries independently by unsigned bits and claim instancewise preservation of singularity.']
- **limit-principal-rank-symmetry** (hypothesis): The full-rank principal-submatrix guarantee requires a symmetric matrix over a field. Blocked use: ['Assume that an asymmetric matrix has a nonsingular principal submatrix whose size equals its rank.']
- **limit-corank-one-reduction-cost** (approximation): The corank reduction includes a multiplicity factor and a large-corank remainder; it is an inequality rather than equality of the singularity and corank-one probabilities. Blocked use: ['Discard the multiplicity and remainder when deriving a finite singularity bound.']
- **limit-admissibility-both-conditions** (hypothesis): Admissibility requires both the quadratic-value condition and the inverse-image infinity-norm bound, with the empty-set convention on singular matrices. Blocked use: ['Accept columns using only the quadratic condition.', 'Form an inverse for a singular starting matrix.']
- **limit-typical-is-probabilistic** (formal_scope): Typical estimates allow an exceptional event and are uniform only in the stated dimension window. Nonsingular-only typical statements count failure together with nonsingularity. Blocked use: ['Treat a typical estimate as a deterministic guarantee for every nonsingular binary matrix.', 'Use a typical estimate outside its dimension window without further justification.']
- **limit-fixed-precision-before-limit** (hypothesis): The exponents governing precision and height are fixed before the reference dimension tends to infinity. Blocked use: ['Choose growing precision exponents and retain the stated asymptotic error estimates.']
- **limit-primitive-lattice-quotient** (hypothesis): The orthogonal quotient and determinant factorization require the removed sublattice to be primitive. Determinants and duals are taken in the relevant real spans. Blocked use: ['Apply the abstract-quotient identification to an unsaturated sublattice.', 'Compute ambient full-dimensional determinants for lower-rank lattices.']
- **limit-reverse-minkowski-all-sublattices** (hypothesis): The Gaussian-mass bound assumes the determinant lower bound for every sublattice, rather than only for the lattice itself. Blocked use: ['Infer the Gaussian-mass estimate from a total determinant bound alone.', 'Infer a cryptographic security guarantee from the Gaussian-mass estimate.']
- **limit-robust-rational-short-image** (hypothesis): The robust-image determinant estimate requires robustness on the affine span, an injective rational affine map, and the stated bound on image differences. Blocked use: ['Apply the determinant estimate to a nonrobust subset.', 'Assume discreteness for unrestricted irrational image differences.', 'Drop the metric bound on image differences.']
- **limit-low-height-cover-effectivity** (nonconstructive): The low-height cover concerns rational subspaces satisfying the height cutoff for sufficiently large reference dimension. Its size bound supplies neither a concrete threshold nor a runtime contract. Blocked use: ['Treat the cover theorem as an efficient implemented enumeration procedure.', 'Transfer simultaneous rank estimates to arbitrary subspaces outside the rational height regime.']
- **limit-inverse-bilinear-restriction** (formal_scope): The inverse-rank corollary concerns the restriction of a bilinear form and requires a typical nonsingular starting matrix and a rational subspace in the stated height range. Blocked use: ['Read the conclusion as the rank of the inverse linear map restricted into the full ambient space.', 'Apply the inverse-form bound without nonsingularity or the height condition.']
- **limit-small-spectrum-not-conditioning** (formal_scope): The small-spectrum proposition bounds the number of eigenvalues in its interval, counted with multiplicity. It does not assert that the interval contains no eigenvalues. Blocked use: ['Use the eigenvalue-count estimate as a lower bound on the least singular value of an individual matrix.']
- **limit-colored-pairing-scope** (hypothesis): The pairing-image bound requires isotropic elements in a single overall color. The coloring relies on a perfect symmetric pairing and its orthogonal primary decomposition. Blocked use: ['Apply the monochromatic image-order bound to an arbitrary mixture of colors.', 'Substitute an arbitrary finite-group pairing for the perfect symmetric pairing.']
- **limit-gated-paired-deletion** (hypothesis): Gated deletion starts from a nonsingular symmetric binary matrix. The paired construction selects an inverse entry of maximal absolute value to obtain the norm constraints, and the empty minor uses the stated conventions. Blocked use: ['Choose an arbitrary inverse off-diagonal pair and claim admissibility from the proof.', 'Start the inverse-based deletion procedure at a singular matrix.', 'Reject the valid empty complementary minor because it has no coordinates.']
- **limit-retention-membership-necessary** (formal_scope): The retention lattice gives a necessary membership condition for retaining the selected positive layers; the lemma does not state the converse. Blocked use: ['Treat lattice membership as a sufficient certificate that every selected layer is retained.', 'Ignore the supplied quotient map when constructing the lattice.']
- **limit-simultaneous-cube-event** (hypothesis): Adaptive selection of an intermediate lattice is permitted on the single typical event, but the lattice must contain the matrix column lattice and be contained in the integer lattice. Blocked use: ['Use the cube-coset concentration bound for an unrelated lattice.', 'Replace the typical-event requirement by a guarantee for every supplied matrix.']
- **limit-witness-direct-sum** (hypothesis): Witness pricing requires nonempty robust cube subsets, differences in the intermediate lattice, a direct sum of their difference spans, and the stated small coordinate-coset intersection. Blocked use: ['Price overlapping difference spans as independent pieces.', 'Apply the witness inequality without the coordinate intersection bound.']
- **limit-certificate-exposure-and-prefixes** (hypothesis): Certificate pricing requires conditional uniformity of the tested column, categories made of whole plateaus, prefix marks on each plateau, and classes constructed before representative and target selection. Blocked use: ['Condition on information that biases the tested column while retaining the bound.', 'Choose arbitrary nonprefix marks.', 'Construct classes after choosing target weights and invoke the uniform class guarantee.']
- **limit-budget-compensation-existence** (nonconstructive): Budget compensation requires both endpoint budgets and proves existence of suitable real target weights. The compact maximization argument does not specify a finite exact solver for arbitrary real inputs. Blocked use: ['Drop either endpoint budget.', 'Advertise the existence proof as a solver with certified runtime.']
- **limit-potential-step-gate** (hypothesis): The local potential estimate imposes typicality on the starting matrix and includes both nonsingularity of the destination and the extension gate in its expectation. Blocked use: ['Remove the paired-extension gate from the local moment estimate.', 'Apply the estimate from an arbitrary atypical starting matrix.']
- **limit-heavy-retention-range** (hypothesis): The heavy-retention estimate needs no typicality hypothesis, but still requires a nonsingular exposed starting matrix, a compatible quotient map, conditional uniformity, the prescribed heavy-height cutoff, and the stated range of layer mass. Blocked use: ['Apply the heavy-retention estimate below its lower mass endpoint.', 'Use a tested column whose law has changed after exposure.']
- **limit-fixed-forward-path-exposure** (formal_scope): Backward selection supplies a cover by fixed index paths. Probability estimates use forward exposure on each fixed path and impose future requirements through indicators rather than conditioning on the backward selection. Blocked use: ['Condition the forward random process on a path chosen from the terminal matrix and assume its new columns remain uniform.']
- **limit-finite-deletion-coverage** (formal_scope): Finite exact deletion checks concern a deterministic ingredient. They do not certify the asymptotic singularity theorem, its typical events, or its potential moment. Blocked use: ['Promote bounded deletion tests to certification of the sharp asymptotic rate.', 'Claim universal paired-complement coverage from tests using only an empty paired complement.']

          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ have rank $r$ and span $E$, and let $K\\subset L$ be a primitive sublattice of rank $k$, with $F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}K$. The Euclidean realization $$L/K:=\\pi_{F^\\perp}(L)$$ is a lattice of rank $r-k$, canonically isomorphic to the abstract quotient, and $$\\det L=\\det K\\,\\det(L/K),\\qquad\n \\det L^*=(\\det L)^{-1}.$$ The dual identities are $$(L/K)^*=L^*\\cap F^\\perp,\\qquad\n K^*=\\pi_F(L^*),$$ where $\\pi_F$ is orthogonal projection onto $F$. For any sublattice $M\\subset L/K$, its full preimage $\\widetilde M=\\{x\\in L:\\pi_{F^\\perp}x\\in M\\}$ has rank $k+\\mathop{\\mathrm{rank}}M$ and satisfies $$\\det\\widetilde M=\\det K\\,\\det M.$$ These assertions include $k=0$, $k=r$, and $r=0$, with all rank-zero determinants equal to one.",

          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }

      "blocked_use": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gated-deletion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a nonsingular symmetric $r\\times r$ matrix with entries in $\\{0,1\\}$, where $r\\ge1$. Either a principal submatrix obtained by deleting one coordinate is nonsingular, or there are distinct coordinates $i,j$ such that, with $I=[r]\\setminus\\{i,j\\}$, $$C=B_{I,I}\\quad\\hbox{is nonsingular},\\qquad\n v_1=B_{I,i}\\in\\mathcal S(C),\\quad v_2=B_{I,j}\\in\\mathcal S(C).$$ For the empty minor we use determinant one, and $\\mathcal S$ contains its unique empty column.",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-gated-deletion",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-gated-deletion</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The cofactor identity gives $$\\det B_{[r]\\setminus\\{i\\},[r]\\setminus\\{i\\}}\n   =\\det B\\,(B^{-1})_{ii}.$$ Thus a nonzero diagonal entry of $B^{-1}$ gives the first alternative. Suppose instead that all diagonal entries of $B^{-1}$ vanish. Since $B^{-1}$ is nonsingular, it has a nonzero off-diagonal entry. Choose $i,j$ to maximize the absolute value of an entry of $B^{-1}$, and write $\\kappa=(B^{-1})_{ij}\\ne0$. Then $$(B^{-1})_{\\{i,j\\},\\{i,j\\}}=\n \\begin{pmatrix}0&\\kappa\\\\ \\kappa&0\\end{pmatrix}.$$ Jacobi’s complementary minor identity gives $$\\det C=\\det B\\,\n          \\det (B^{-1})_{\\{i,j\\},\\{i,j\\}}\n        =-\\kappa^2\\det B\\ne0.$$\n\nOrder the coordinates with $I$ first and write $$B=\\begin{pmatrix}C&V\\\\ V^{\\mathsf T}&D\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad V=(v_1\\ v_2),\\qquad\n T=D-V^{\\mathsf T}C^{-1}V.$$ Block inversion shows that $$T^{-1}=(B^{-1})_{\\{i,j\\},\\{i,j\\}}\n       =\\begin{pmatrix}0&\\kappa\\\\ \\kappa&0\\end{pmatrix},\n \\qquad\n (B^{-1})_{I,\\{i,j\\}}=-C^{-1}VT^{-1}.$$ The diagonal of $T$ is therefore zero. Its two diagonal equations give $v_a^{\\mathsf T}C^{-1}v_a=D_{aa}\\in\\{0,1\\}$ for $a=1,2$. Moreover, the two columns of the displayed inverse block are $-\\kappa C^{-1}v_2$ and $-\\kappa C^{-1}v_1$. Every entry in that block has absolute value at most $|\\kappa|$, by the choice of $i,j$. Hence $\\|C^{-1}v_a\\|_\\infty\\le1$ for $a=1,2$. These two facts are exactly the defining conditions for $v_1,v_2\\in\\mathcal S(C)$. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-239.1"
        }
      ],

Suppose $S_i-S_i\\subset K$, and put $W_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{span}}(S_i-S_i)$. Suppose also that $W=\\sum_i W_i$ is a direct sum.           "anchor": "stmt-prop-certificate-pricing",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $A>0$ and at most two positive constants $w_i$. Typically for nonsingular binary matrices $C$ of size $m\\in\\mathcal I_N$, the following assertion holds. The typical set is obtained by imposing Proposition <a href=\"#prop:cube-concentration\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:cube-concentration\">31</a> at a sufficiently large fixed precision depending on $A$ and these constants.*\n\n*Let $\\mathcal F$ denote exposed information which fixes $C$, a quotient map $G_C\\twoheadrightarrow G$, and the data described next. Suppose that, conditionally on $\\mathcal F$, a tested column $v$ is uniform on $\\{0,1\\}^m$. Partition the layers of $G$ into at most two categories, each a union of whole plateaus. In category $i$, assume that every height is at least $w_i$; only $w_i=1,2$ will be needed. The partition is part of the exposed data. In the quotient of $G$ by the image of $v$, an outcome may mark some of the retained layers. Require that the marked layers on each plateau form a prefix. The marks may also depend on other random bits in the experiment.*\n\n\n*Let $\\mathcal A$ be any subevent of this experiment. There is a partition of $\\mathcal A$ into at most $2^{N\\ell^{-A}}$ classes, constructed before choosing a representative or any target weights, with the following property. Take any nonempty class $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$, any representative outcome in it, and write $s_i$ and $u_i$ for the representative’s sums of the weights $\\nu_p$ of the marked and unmarked layers in category $i$. For any choices $0\\le x_i\\le s_i$, $$\\label{eq:certificate-pricing}\n \\Pr(\\mathcal A_\\alpha\\mid\\mathcal F)\n \\le 2^{-N\\left(\n c\\left(\\sum_i w_i x_i\\right)\n       -\\sum_i\\mathcal E(s_i,u_i,x_i)-\\ell^{-A}\\right)}.$$ Here $\\mathcal A_\\alpha$ already includes the restriction to $\\mathcal A$; the probability is conditioned only on the exposed information. The class count and the estimate are uniform in that information, the quotient, the subevent, and the representative. In particular, the exposed information may include a preceding column that fixes $G$, provided the tested column remains conditionally uniform.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>A</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> be a symmetric $`n\times n`$ matrix whose entries on and above the diagonal are independent uniform signs. We prove

$`\displaystyle \Pr(\det A_n=0)=\left(\frac12+o(1)\right)^n.`$

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| [stmt-lem-sign-bit](#stmt-lem-sign-bit) | lemma | `lem:sign-bit` | 2 | For $n\ge2$, $$\Pr(\det A_n=0)=\Pr(\det\mathbf B_{n-1}=0).$$* |
| [stmt-lem-principal-rank](#stmt-lem-principal-rank) | lemma | `lem:principal-rank` | 3 | Let $M$ be a symmetric matrix over a field, of rank $r$. |
| [stmt-lem-corank-tail](#stmt-lem-corank-tail) | lemma | `lem:corank-tail` | 4 | Let $\mathbb K$ be any field, and regard the bits of $\mathbf B_m$ as elements of $\mathbb K$. |
| [stmt-lem-corank-reduction](#stmt-lem-corank-reduction) | lemma | `lem:corank-reduction` | 5 | For the binary model in dimension $N$, |
| [stmt-lem-column-reduction](#stmt-lem-column-reduction) | lemma | `lem:column-reduction` | 6 | One has |
| [stmt-prop-admissible-potential](#stmt-prop-admissible-potential) | proposition | `prop:admissible-potential` | 7 | For every fixed $A>0$, typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, $$&#124;\mathcal S(B)&#124;\le 2^{N(\Psi(B)+\ell^{-A})}.$$* |
| [stmt-prop-path-potential](#stmt-prop-path-potential) | proposition | `prop:path-potential` | 8 | For a binary matrix of size $N-1$, |
| [stmt-lem-lattice-quotient](#stmt-lem-lattice-quotient) | lemma | `lem:lattice-quotient` | 9 | Let $L$ have rank $r$ and span $E$, and let $K\subset L$ be a primitive sublattice of rank $k$, with $F=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}K$. |
| [stmt-lem-height-submodularity](#stmt-lem-height-submodularity) | lemma | `lem:height-submodularity` | 10 | For rational subspaces $U,V\subset\mathbb R^m$, $$H(U+V)H(U\cap V)\le H(U)H(V).$$ Consequently $h(U+V)+h(U\cap V)\le h(U)+h(V)$. |
| [stmt-lem-normal-height](#stmt-lem-normal-height) | lemma | `lem:normal-height` | 11 | Let $V\subset U\subset\mathbb R^m$ be rational subspaces, and put $\Lambda=\Lambda_U^*$. |
| [stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski](#stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski) | theorem | `thm:reverse-minkowski` | 12 | Let $L$ be a lattice of rank $j\ge1$, and let $T>0$. |
| [stmt-lem-gaussian-count](#stmt-lem-gaussian-count) | lemma | `lem:gaussian-count` | 13 | Let $L$ be a rank-$j$ lattice. |
| [stmt-lem-det-minimizer](#stmt-lem-det-minimizer) | lemma | `lem:det-minimizer` | 14 | Let $L$ be a lattice and let $T>0$. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $A_n$ be a symmetric $n\times n$ random matrix whose entries on and above the diagonal are independent and uniform on $\{-1,1\}$. We study the probability that $A_n$ is singular. A specified pair of rows is equal with probability $2^{-n}$: the $n-2$ comparisons away from the two diagonal positions and the two remaining diagonal conditions are independent. Thus singularity already occurs at this exponential scale.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For the symmetric Bernoulli matrix $A_n$, $$\Pr(\det A_n=0)=\left(\frac12+o(1)\right)^n
                =2^{-n+o(n)}
 \qquad\text{as }n\to\infty.$$*

</div>

The corresponding problem for a matrix with all $n^2$ entries independent has a long history. Komlós proved that a matrix with independent uniform $0/1$ entries is nonsingular with probability tending to one <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Komlos](#ref-Komlos)]. Tikhomirov proved the sharp rate $(1/2+o(1))^n$ for independent uniform signs <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Tikhomirov](#ref-Tikhomirov)]. Symmetry changes the exposure of the matrix: revealing a row also reveals entries in all later rows. Arguments based on independent rows therefore need a different source of anti-concentration.

Costello, Tao, and Vu proved that a random symmetric Bernoulli matrix is nonsingular with probability tending to one, obtaining the bound $O(n^{-1/8+\delta})$ for every fixed $\delta>0$ <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[CTV](#ref-CTV)]. Their proof developed quadratic Littlewood–Offord estimates to handle the dependence between a row and its matching column. Nguyen improved the bound to $O_C(n^{-C})$ for every fixed $C>0$ <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Nguyen](#ref-Nguyen)], and Vershynin obtained a bound of the form $\exp(-n^c)$ for the sign model, as a special case of his theorem for a class of subgaussian ensembles <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Vershynin](#ref-Vershynin)]. Further quantitative advances for this model gave bounds of the forms $\exp(-c n^{1/4}\sqrt{\log n})$ <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[FerberJain](#ref-FerberJain)], $\exp(-c\sqrt n)$ <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CamposMattosMorrisMorrison](#ref-CamposMattosMorrisMorrison)], $\exp(-c\sqrt n(\log n)^{1/4})$ <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[JainSahSawhney](#ref-JainSahSawhney)], and $\exp(-c\sqrt{n\log n})$ <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CJMSRevisited](#ref-CJMSRevisited)]. Campos, Jenssen, Michelen, and Sahasrabudhe proved an exponential bound $\exp(-cn)$, using inverse Littlewood–Offord estimates for conditioned random walks and the method of inversion of randomness <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[SymmetricExponential](#ref-SymmetricExponential)]. Related work gives quantitative control of the least singular value <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[SymmetricLeastSingular](#ref-SymmetricLeastSingular)] and of larger rank deficiencies <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[HanRank](#ref-HanRank)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> identifies the leading exponential rate for the specified sign model.

Our proof begins with an exact reduction to symmetric matrices of bits. A Schur complement converts the sign matrix in dimension $n$ to a binary matrix in dimension $N=n-1$. A reduction to corank one then turns singularity into a question about appending a new column to a nonsingular matrix $B$. The relevant bit columns $z$ satisfy both $z^{\mathsf T}B^{-1}z\in\{0,1\}$ and $\|B^{-1}z\|_\infty\le1$. It suffices to prove that their expected number is $2^{o(N)}$.

For a typical matrix $B$, the column count is controlled by an arithmetic potential $\Psi(B)\in[0,1]$, defined in Section <a href="#sec:framework" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:framework">2</a>. The discriminant group $G_B=\mathbb Z^m/B\mathbb Z^m$ carries the symmetric pairing $$([x],[y])\longmapsto x^{\mathsf T}B^{-1}y\pmod{\mathbb Z}.$$ Admissible classes have zero self-pairing. We partition them so that pairings within each part have controlled denominators, with the cost expressed in terms of the invariant factors. A robust subset of a large part yields a lattice of controlled covolume on the graph of $B^{-1}$. A cover for rational subspaces of controlled height makes rank estimates simultaneous for the span selected from these columns. Comparing a lower determinant bound from the pairing with an upper bound from typical rank and spectral properties proves the column estimate. The reusable lattice arguments control covolumes and count short vectors after removing a determinant-minimizing sublattice; the Reverse Minkowski Theorem of Regev and Stephens-Davidowitz <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ReverseMinkowski](#ref-ReverseMinkowski)] is applied to its orthogonal quotient.

For a nonsingular extension of width one, the old and new discriminant groups have a common quotient. A kernel count modulo prime powers uses symmetry to restrict the possible layer changes, while retaining prescribed layers forces the new column into an integer lattice. A second application of the lattice tools bounds cube intersections simultaneously for all such lattices; it compares the cost of specifying bases of cube differences with the number of matrix entries they constrain. Counting partial certificates of retained layers and optimizing their size then pays for the increase of $\Psi$ in a conditional exponential moment. For extensions of width two, a gate requires both new columns to be admissible, and the static column estimate directly pays for the probability of this gate.

Finally, every nonsingular matrix admits a path of nonsingular principal minors with steps of width one or two, with the width-two steps satisfying the gate. A separate elementary estimate for retaining high layers, together with a bound on coranks modulo all primes, uses a long initial part of the path to make a large high-layer tail unlikely anywhere in a short terminal window. Outside this exceptional event, if the potential is small at a node in that window, the local conditional estimate controls the terminal moment. Otherwise a bounded auxiliary statistic decreases at every step except those with substantial retention or an expensive width-two gate. Many such steps are necessary, and their conditional probability bounds make this alternative negligible. The long initial part controls the high-layer tail, while the short window limits the accumulation of local errors.

<a id="sec:framework"></a>
### The binary model and the proof framework

We first reduce the singularity probability to the expected size of a set of possible new columns. We then introduce the potential that will control this set and state the two estimates on which the rest of the proof rests.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### From signs to bits

A *binary matrix* of size $m$ is a symmetric $m\times m$ matrix whose entries on and above the diagonal are independent and uniform on $\{0,1\}$. We write $\mathbf B_m$ for a random binary matrix and use ordinary letters for its realizations.

<a id="stmt-lem-sign-bit"></a>
<div id="lem:sign-bit" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *For $n\ge2$, $$\Pr(\det A_n=0)=\Pr(\det\mathbf B_{n-1}=0).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-sign-bit"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-sign-bit</summary>

*Proof.* Put $\epsilon=(A_n)_{11}$, multiply $A_n$ by $\epsilon$, and then apply the diagonal congruence with diagonal $$1,\ \epsilon(A_n)_{12},\ldots,\epsilon(A_n)_{1n}.$$ The resulting first row and column consist of ones. Conditional on the original first row, the lower principal block still has independent uniform signs on and above its diagonal: every one of these signs has only been multiplied by a sign determined by the first row. Its Schur complement at the top left entry is therefore $-2\mathbf B_{n-1}$. The two operations and the Schur complement preserve singularity. ◻

</details>

For the remainder of the proof, $N$ is a reference integer tending to infinity and $$\ell=\log\log N,\qquad
 \mathcal I_N=\{m\in\mathbb Z:N-N^{7/10}\le m\le N\}.$$ The logarithm $\log$ is natural, while $\log_2$ and $\log_N$ have the indicated bases. Every constant exponent of $\ell$ is fixed before $N$ tends to infinity. In particular, $\ell^D/\log N=o(\ell^{-A})$ for all fixed $A,D>0$. We write $[m]=\{1,\ldots,m\}$.

We say that a property of $\mathbf B_m$ *holds typically* if its failure probability is at most $2^{-Nf(N)}$, uniformly for $m\in\mathcal I_N$, for some $f(N)\to\infty$. When the property is stated only for nonsingular matrices, failure means nonsingularity together with failure of the stated conclusion. The typical set may depend on any fixed precision parameters in the statement. A finite intersection of typical properties is again typical.

All unqualified ranks are over $\mathbb R$. We will also use the following fact over finite fields.

<a id="stmt-lem-principal-rank"></a>
<div id="lem:principal-rank" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Principal rank). *Let $M$ be a symmetric matrix over a field, of rank $r$. Then $M$ has a nonsingular principal submatrix of size $r$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-principal-rank"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-principal-rank</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a matrix $X$ whose $r$ columns form a basis of the column space of $M$, and choose a left inverse $T$ of $X$. Since $M$ is symmetric, its row space is the row space of $X^{\mathsf T}$. Thus $$M=X S X^{\mathsf T},\qquad S=TMT^{\mathsf T}.$$ The matrix $S$ is symmetric and has rank $r$. Choose $r$ independent rows of $X$. On those indices the principal submatrix of $M$ is a product of three nonsingular $r\times r$ matrices. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-corank-tail"></a>
<div id="lem:corank-tail" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Corank tail). *Let $\mathbb K$ be any field, and regard the bits of $\mathbf B_m$ as elements of $\mathbb K$. For $1\le k\le m$, $$\Pr\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb K}\mathbf B_m\ge k\bigr)
 \le \sum_{j=k}^{m}\binom mj\,2^{-j(j+1)/2}.$$ Consequently, uniformly for $m\in\mathcal I_N$, $$\Pr\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}_{\mathbb K}\mathbf B_m>N^{3/5}\bigr)
 \le 2^{-\Omega(N^{6/5})}.$$ The implicit constant in the last bound is independent of the field.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-corank-tail"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-corank-tail</summary>

*Proof.* If the rank is $m-j$, Lemma <a href="#lem:principal-rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:principal-rank">3</a> supplies a nonsingular principal submatrix on some $m-j$ indices. Reveal all entries in the corresponding columns. The remaining symmetric $j\times j$ block is forced over $\mathbb K$, since its Schur complement must vanish. Each forced entry has at most one possible bit value, so the conditional probability is at most $2^{-j(j+1)/2}$. Union over the choices of indices and over $j\ge k$. For $j>N^{3/5}$, the factor $2^{-j(j+1)/2}$ dominates the at most $2^m$ choices of indices and the $m$ choices of $j$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-corank-reduction"></a>
<div id="lem:corank-reduction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Reduction to corank one). *For the binary model in dimension $N$, $$\Pr(\det\mathbf B_N=0)
 \le 2^{o(N)}\Pr(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}\mathbf B_N=1)
       +2^{-\Omega(N^{6/5})}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-corank-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-corank-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* Let $B$ have corank $k\ge1$, and write $K=\ker B$. Choose $k-1$ coordinates whose restrictions to $K$ are independent linear functionals. Flip the diagonal bit at each of those coordinates, and write $D$ for the diagonal matrix of the changes. Its selected diagonal entries are all nonzero.

If $(B+D)v=0$, pairing this equation with every $u\in K$ gives $u^{\mathsf T}Dv=0$. The chosen coordinate functionals on $K$ are independent, so the selected coordinates of $v$ are zero. It follows that $Bv=0$. Conversely, every vector in $K$ with these coordinates zero lies in $\ker(B+D)$. This subspace has dimension one. Hence flipping $k-1$ diagonal bits produces a matrix of corank one.

For every matrix with $k\le N^{3/5}$, fix one such choice of coordinates. Each resulting matrix has at most $$\sum_{j\le \lceil N^{3/5}\rceil}\binom Nj
   =2^{O(N^{3/5}\log N)}=2^{o(N)}$$ preimages, because a preimage is recovered by specifying the flipped coordinates. All binary matrices are equally likely. The matrices of larger corank have the probability bounded in Lemma <a href="#lem:corank-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:corank-tail">4</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Admissible columns

For a nonsingular binary matrix $B$ of size $m$, define <a id="eq:admissible-set"></a>$$\label{eq:admissible-set}
 \mathcal S(B)=
 \left\{z\in\{0,1\}^m:
 z^{\mathsf T}B^{-1}z\in\{0,1\},\quad
 \|B^{-1}z\|_\infty\le1\right\}.$$ Set $\mathcal S(B)=\varnothing$ when $B$ is singular. These are the columns that can occur when a corank-one matrix is reduced by deleting a largest coordinate of its kernel vector.

<a id="stmt-lem-column-reduction"></a>
<div id="lem:column-reduction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *One has $$\Pr(\mathop{\mathrm{corank}}\mathbf B_N=1)
 \le N\,2^{-N}\,\mathbb E|\mathcal S(\mathbf B_{N-1})|.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-column-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-column-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose a symmetric matrix $M$ of size $N$ has corank one, and choose $0\ne v\in\ker M$. Delete an index $i$ for which $|v_i|$ is largest. The resulting principal submatrix $B$ is nonsingular. Indeed, the adjugate of $M$ is a nonzero scalar multiple of $vv^{\mathsf T}$, so its $i$-th diagonal entry is nonzero.

Write $z$ for the column at $i$ on the remaining indices and $a$ for its diagonal bit. The kernel equation and the Schur complement give $$B^{-1}z=-v_{[N]\setminus\{i\}}/v_i,\qquad
 a=z^{\mathsf T}B^{-1}z.$$ Thus $z\in\mathcal S(B)$. For any fixed deleted index, conditional on $B$, the pair $(z,a)$ is uniform on $\{0,1\}^{N-1}\times\{0,1\}$. For each $z\in\mathcal S(B)$, the displayed equality fixes $a$. The probability for that index is therefore $2^{-N}\mathbb E|\mathcal S(\mathbf B_{N-1})|$. Union over $i$. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:column-target"></a>
It remains to prove $$\label{eq:column-target}
 \mathbb E|\mathcal S(\mathbf B_{N-1})|\le 2^{o(N)}.$$ Indeed, Lemmas <a href="#lem:corank-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:corank-reduction">5</a> and <a href="#lem:column-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:column-reduction">6</a> then give the upper bound $2^{-N+o(N)}$ for the binary singularity probability. A specified row of $\mathbf B_N$ is zero with probability $2^{-N}$, which gives the matching lower bound.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### A potential on the discriminant group

For nonsingular $B$, the finite abelian group $$G_B=\mathbb Z^m/B\mathbb Z^m$$ has order $|\det B|$. It records the possible denominators of $B^{-1}z$ for integer $z$. We now give a numerical measure of its invariant factors.

<a id="eq:group-order"></a>
For a finite abelian group $G$, write its $p$-primary part as $$G_p\cong\bigoplus_{i\ge1}\mathbb Z/p^{b_{p,i}}\mathbb Z,\qquad
 b_{p,1}\ge b_{p,2}\ge\cdots\ge0,$$ where only finitely many exponents are positive. For $e\ge1$, put $$k_G(p,e)=\#\{i:b_{p,i}\ge e\},\qquad
 \nu_p=\frac{\log_N p}{N}.$$ We call $(p,e)$ with $k_G(p,e)>0$ a *layer*, its integer $k_G(p,e)$ its *height*, and $\nu_p$ its *weight*. All sums over layers below are finite. The identity $$\label{eq:group-order}
 \sum_{p,e} k_G(p,e)\nu_p=\frac{\log|G|}{N\log N}$$ follows from $\sum_e k_G(p,e)=\sum_i b_{p,i}$. Hadamard’s inequality gives $|\det B|\le N^{N/2}$ for a binary matrix of size $m\le N$. Hence the sum in Equation <a href="#eq:group-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:group-order">[eq:group-order]</a> is at most $1/2$ for $G_B$ and for every quotient of $G_B$.

<a id="eq:potential"></a>
Write $k_B(p,e)=k_{G_B}(p,e)$, set $\beta=7/5$, and define $$\label{eq:potential}
 \Psi(B)=\sum_{p,e}\nu_p\,\phi(k_B(p,e)),\qquad
 \phi(0)=\phi(1)=0,\quad
 \phi(2)=\beta,\quad
 \phi(k)=2k\quad(k\ge3).$$ In particular $0\le\Psi(B)\le1$, since $0\le\phi(k)\le2k$. We set $\Psi(B)=0$ for singular $B$ when an expression also contains a nonsingularity indicator.

For example, a $p$-primary part with positive cyclic exponents $(5,3,1)$ has $$(k_B(p,1),\ldots,k_B(p,5))=(3,2,2,1,1),$$ so this primary part contributes $(6+2\beta)\nu_p$ to $\Psi(B)$. Figure <a href="#fig:layers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:layers">1</a> depicts these layers. The coefficient $\beta=7/5$ exceeds the coefficient $5/4$ in the height-two term of the color-cost bound in Section <a href="#sec:discriminant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:discriminant">6</a>, while satisfying the scalar budget inequality in Section <a href="#sec:potential-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:potential-step">10</a>.

The potential is chosen to support two estimates. The first relates the number of admissible columns to the arithmetic structure of $B$.

<a id="stmt-prop-admissible-potential"></a>
<div id="prop:admissible-potential" class="proposition">

**Proposition 7** (Admissible-column estimate). *For every fixed $A>0$, typically for nonsingular binary matrices $B$ of size $m\in\mathcal I_N$, $$|\mathcal S(B)|\le 2^{N(\Psi(B)+\ell^{-A})}.$$*

</div>

The second estimate controls the potential in expectation.

<a id="stmt-prop-path-potential"></a>
<div id="prop:path-potential" class="proposition">

**Proposition 8** (Potential moment). *For a binary matrix of size $N-1$, $$\mathbb E\!\left[\mathbf 1_{\{\det\mathbf B_{N-1}\ne0\}}\,
            2^{N\Psi(\mathbf B_{N-1})}\right]\le 2^{o(N)}.$$*

</div>

These propositions imply Equation <a href="#eq:column-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:column-target">[eq:column-target]</a>. Indeed, fix $A>0$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:admissible-potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:admissible-potential">7</a>. On its typical set use that estimate, and on the exceptional set use $|\mathcal S(B)|\le2^N$. For a suitable $f(N)\to\infty$, $$\mathbb E|\mathcal S(\mathbf B_{N-1})|
 \le 2^{N\ell^{-A}}
     \mathbb E\!\left[\mathbf 1_{\{\det\mathbf B_{N-1}\ne0\}}\,
                2^{N\Psi(\mathbf B_{N-1})}\right]
       +2^{N-Nf(N)}
 =2^{o(N)}.$$

Sections <a href="#sec:lattice-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lattice-tools">3</a>–<a href="#sec:discriminant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:discriminant">6</a> prove the static estimate in Proposition <a href="#prop:admissible-potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:admissible-potential">7</a> by combining the discriminant pairing with lattice, rank, and spectral estimates. Sections <a href="#sec:minor-moves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:minor-moves">7</a>–<a href="#sec:potential-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:potential-step">10</a> prove a local conditional estimate for extensions: certificate pricing handles width one, while Proposition <a href="#prop:admissible-potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:admissible-potential">7</a> directly pays for the gate at width two. Section <a href="#sec:minor-paths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:minor-paths">11</a> adds a separate high-layer tail argument and combines these estimates along paths to prove Proposition <a href="#prop:path-potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:path-potential">8</a>.

<a id="sec:lattice-tools"></a>
### Lattice tools and robust subsets

The lattice estimates in this section serve two purposes. They count short vectors after removing directions of small determinant, and they show that a robust collection of cube points has a difference span of small height. We first fix the determinant conventions needed for both uses.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Determinants, quotients, and rational height

A lattice $L$ is a discrete subgroup of a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, considered inside its real span $E=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}L$. Its rank is $\dim E$. If $b_1,\ldots,b_r$ is a basis of $L$, its *determinant* is the Euclidean covolume $$\det L=\det\bigl(\langle b_i,b_j\rangle_{i,j=1}^r\bigr)^{1/2}.$$ We set $\det\{0\}=1$. Sublattices may have smaller rank. The saturation of a sublattice $M\subset L$ is $\overline M=L\cap\mathop{\mathrm{span}}M$; it is a lattice of the same rank, and $$\det M=[\overline M:M]\det\overline M.$$ We call $M$ *primitive*, or *saturated*, when $M=\overline M$. A primitive sublattice has a basis that extends to a basis of $L$. The dual lattice is always taken in the span: $$L^*=\{y\in E:\langle y,x\rangle\in\mathbb Z\text{ for all }x\in L\}.$$ In particular, the dual of the rank-zero lattice is the rank-zero lattice.

<a id="stmt-lem-lattice-quotient"></a>
<div id="lem:lattice-quotient" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Orthogonal lattice quotients). *Let $L$ have rank $r$ and span $E$, and let $K\subset L$ be a primitive sublattice of rank $k$, with $F=\mathop{\mathrm{span}}K$. Write $\pi_{F^\perp}$ for orthogonal projection from $E$ onto $E\cap F^\perp$. The Euclidean realization $$L/K:=\pi_{F^\perp}(L)$$ is a lattice of rank $r-k$, canonically isomorphic to the abstract quotient, and $$\det L=\det K\,\det(L/K),\qquad
 \det L^*=(\det L)^{-1}.$$ The dual identities are $$(L/K)^*=L^*\cap F^\perp,\qquad
 K^*=\pi_F(L^*),$$ where $\pi_F$ is orthogonal projection onto $F$. For any sublattice $M\subset L/K$, its full preimage $\widetilde M=\{x\in L:\pi_{F^\perp}x\in M\}$ has rank $k+\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M$ and satisfies $$\det\widetilde M=\det K\,\det M.$$ These assertions include $k=0$, $k=r$, and $r=0$, with all rank-zero determinants equal to one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-lattice-quotient"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-lattice-quotient</summary>

*Proof.* Extend a basis $b_1,\ldots,b_k$ of $K$ to a basis $b_1,\ldots,b_r$ of $L$. The projected vectors $\pi_{F^\perp}b_{k+1},\ldots,\pi_{F^\perp}b_r$ form a basis of the projected lattice. Splitting Euclidean volume into the factor in $F$ and the factor orthogonal to $F$ gives the first determinant identity. The Gram matrix of the dual basis is the inverse Gram matrix, giving the identity for $\det L^*$.

For $y\in E\cap F^\perp$, the equality $\langle y,\pi_{F^\perp}x\rangle=\langle y,x\rangle$ gives $(L/K)^*=L^*\cap F^\perp$. Projection of $L^*$ to $F$ is contained in $K^*$. Conversely, for $w\in K^*$, prescribe on the basis of $L$ the integer values $\langle w,b_i\rangle$ for $i\le k$ and zero for $i>k$. The representing vector belongs to $L^*$, and its projection to $F$ is $w$. This proves the second dual identity. Finally, lift a basis of $M$ and adjoin the basis of $K$. The resulting basis of $\widetilde M$ gives its rank and determinant by the same volume factorization. Empty bases give all the rank-zero cases. ◻

</details>

A subspace $W\subset\mathbb R^m$ is *rational* if it is spanned by integer vectors. Its integer lattice $\Lambda_W=W\cap\mathbb Z^m$ has rank $\dim W$, and its *height* is $$H(W)=\det\Lambda_W.$$ Thus $H(\{0\})=H(\mathbb R^m)=1$. We will also write $h(W)=\log_N H(W)$.

<a id="stmt-lem-height-submodularity"></a>
<div id="lem:height-submodularity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (Height submodularity). *For rational subspaces $U,V\subset\mathbb R^m$, $$H(U+V)H(U\cap V)\le H(U)H(V).$$ Consequently $h(U+V)+h(U\cap V)\le h(U)+h(V)$. Every rational subspace has height at least one, and, for fixed $m$ and $C<\infty$, there are only finitely many rational subspaces $W\subset\mathbb R^m$ with $H(W)\le C$. These statements include rank-zero subspaces.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-height-submodularity"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-height-submodularity</summary>

*Proof.* Put $J=U\cap V$, and project orthogonally off $J$. The lattice $\Lambda_J=\Lambda_U\cap\Lambda_V$ is primitive in each of $\Lambda_U,\Lambda_V,\Lambda_U+\Lambda_V$. For the last assertion, if $u+v\in J$ with $u\in\Lambda_U$ and $v\in\Lambda_V$, then $u,v\in J$. The projected spans of $U$ and $V$ intersect trivially. If $\overline\Lambda_U,\overline\Lambda_V$ denote their projected lattices, the union of bases of these lattices is therefore a basis of their sum. Orthogonally projecting the second set of basis vectors off the span of the first can only decrease its volume. Hence $$\det(\overline\Lambda_U+\overline\Lambda_V)
 \le \det\overline\Lambda_U\,\det\overline\Lambda_V.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:lattice-quotient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lattice-quotient">9</a> now gives $$\det(\Lambda_U+\Lambda_V)\det\Lambda_J
 \le \det\Lambda_U\,\det\Lambda_V.$$ The saturation of $\Lambda_U+\Lambda_V$ is $\Lambda_{U+V}$, whose determinant is no larger. This proves the height inequality, and taking $\log_N$ proves its additive form.

For a rank-$r$ rational subspace, wedge a basis of $\Lambda_W$. The resulting vector in $\bigwedge^r\mathbb Z^m$ is determined up to sign, has Euclidean norm $H(W)$, and determines its supporting subspace $W$. Its integer coordinates are not all zero, so its norm is at least one. A bounded ball contains finitely many vectors of $\bigwedge^r\mathbb Z^m$, and there are only $m+1$ possible ranks. This proves the finiteness assertion. For $r=0$, the exterior vector is $1$ and there is just the zero subspace. ◻

</details>

For a coordinate vector $e_i$, both possible intersections $U\cap\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(e_i)$ have height one. Lemma <a href="#lem:height-submodularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:height-submodularity">10</a> therefore also gives $H(U+\mathop{\mathrm{span}}(e_i))\le H(U)$. The next identity expresses height ratios as determinants of normal lattices; it will transfer height minimization to bounds on all sublattices of a dual lattice.

<a id="stmt-lem-normal-height"></a>
<div id="lem:normal-height" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Normal lattices and height). *Let $V\subset U\subset\mathbb R^m$ be rational subspaces, and put $\Lambda=\Lambda_U^*$. The primitive normal lattice $$\mathcal N_U(V)=\Lambda\cap V^\perp$$ has rank $\dim U-\dim V$, and $$\pi_V(\Lambda)=\Lambda_V^*,\qquad
 \det\mathcal N_U(V)=\frac{H(V)}{H(U)}.$$ More generally, for any sublattice $M\subset\Lambda$, set $V_M=U\cap(\mathop{\mathrm{span}}M)^\perp$. Then $V_M$ is rational and $$\det M=[\mathcal N_U(V_M):M]\frac{H(V_M)}{H(U)}
 \ge \frac1{H(U)}.$$ For $V=U$ the normal lattice has rank zero and determinant one; for $V=\{0\}$ it is $\Lambda_U^*$. If $M=\{0\}$, the displayed index is one and the determinant formula gives one.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-normal-height"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-normal-height</summary>

*Proof.* The lattice $\Lambda_V$ is primitive in $\Lambda_U$, so the second dual identity in Lemma <a href="#lem:lattice-quotient" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:lattice-quotient">9</a> gives $\pi_V(\Lambda_U^*)=\Lambda_V^*$. Its kernel is $\mathcal N_U(V)$, a primitive sublattice of the asserted rank. Factoring the determinant of $\Lambda_U^*$ along this kernel gives $$\frac1{H(U)}
 =\det\mathcal N_U(V)\,\det\Lambda_V^*
 =\frac{\det\mathcal N_U(V)}{H(V)}.$$

An integer basis matrix for $\Lambda_U$ gives a rational basis for its dual by inverting its Gram matrix. Thus $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}M$, and hence $V_M$, is rational. The saturation of $M$ in $\Lambda$ is exactly $\mathcal N_U(V_M)$. The index formula for determinants and the first part of the lemma give the final identity; the inequality follows from $H(V_M)\ge1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Gaussian counts and determinant minimization

For a lattice $L$ and $s>0$, define its Gaussian mass by $$\rho_s(L)=\sum_{x\in L}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\pi\|x\|^2}{s^2}\right).$$ In rank zero this mass equals one. The following is the scaled form of the Reverse Minkowski Theorem of Regev and Stephens-Davidowitz <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ReverseMinkowski](#ref-ReverseMinkowski), Theorem 1.2].

<a id="stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski"></a>
<div id="thm:reverse-minkowski" class="theorem">

**Theorem 12** (Reverse Minkowski Theorem). *Let $L$ be a lattice of rank $j\ge1$, and let $T>0$. Suppose that $$\det M\ge T^{\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}M}\qquad\text{for every sublattice }M\subset L.$$ With $\tau_j=10(\log j+2)$, one has $$\rho_{T/\tau_j}(L)\le\frac32.$$ For a rank-zero lattice the Gaussian mass is one at every scale, so the corresponding assertion is immediate.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-reverse-minkowski</summary>

*Proof.* Apply the cited theorem to $T^{-1}L$ in its Euclidean span. Every rank-$t$ sublattice of $T^{-1}L$ has determinant at least one, and $\rho_{1/\tau_j}(T^{-1}L)=\rho_{T/\tau_j}(L)$. ◻

</details>
